## [FARE Detects Bait-and-Switch Swaps in Certified AI Image Generators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30982) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces FARE (Forensic Acceptance Region Estimation), a method that enrolls a certified image generator by training on its sampled images and then determines whether any later generated image is consistent with that enrolled generator using only the image itself. FARE amplifies generator-specific forensic artifacts during training by mining hard samples that tighten the acceptance region, and it reportedly outperforms existing baselines at strict operating points across generator swaps, including similar model versions and variants. This addresses a novel AI governance problem: providers can pass certification with one generator and then silently deploy a cheaper, lower-quality one, undermining public trust and safety in high-stakes domains. A practical forensic tool that works without access to model weights or architecture could strengthen accountability for opaque generative AI APIs. FARE relies on image generator-specific artifacts previously proposed for forensic applications and requires only certified-only enrollment, meaning training and calibration use images from a single certified generator. The abstract is concise and lacks experimental details, but the method is reported to remain effective under the exact-model and decision-only attacks evaluated in the work.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Modern AI image generators are often deployed as opaque APIs, where customers can query the service but cannot inspect model weights or architecture. This opacity creates an integrity auditing challenge at deployment time, since a provider could swap the underlying model after certification. FARE builds on image forensics research that identifies generator-specific artifacts, using them to define an acceptance region for the certified generator. The approach is related to broader efforts in AI-generated image detection, such as bias-free training paradigms that focus on generator-specific artifacts rather than dataset biases.

## [Linear Representation Hypothesis Extended to Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30996) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.30996) develops a theoretical, signature-based formulation of the linear representation hypothesis (LRH) for vision-language-action (VLA) models, unifying representations and policies to handle the dynamical nature of embodied interaction. The authors establish that representations exist from which the future evolution of a physical quantity of interest (QoI) under a candidate action trajectory can be recovered via linear probing, and they introduce a signature generalized linear model for stochastic action chunks that enables linear steering. They verify the predicted linear probing and steering mechanisms in a planar control-affine navigation experiment using an explicit oracle representation. This work extends the linear representation hypothesis — a standard tool for interpreting large language models — into the embodied VLA setting, where representations and actions are coupled through system dynamics. If validated at scale, it could give robotics researchers a principled way to probe, monitor, and steer the internal representations of VLA policies, which matters for safety, controllability, and interpretability of embodied AI systems. The formulation is theoretical and signature-based, using the signature of a path to capture the dynamical coupling between representation and policy, and it introduces a signature generalized linear model for stochastic action chunks. The empirical validation is limited to a single planar control-affine navigation experiment with an oracle representation, so the approach has not yet been demonstrated on real robots or large pretrained VLA models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: The linear representation hypothesis (LRH) is the idea, central to mechanistic interpretability, that high-level semantic concepts are encoded as linear directions in the internal activations of neural networks, so they can be read out or modified with linear probes. Vision-language-action (VLA) models, pioneered by Google DeepMind's RT-2 in 2023, are multimodal foundation models that take images and text instructions and directly output low-level robot actions. Unlike static semantic attributes in LLMs, a physical quantity of interest in a VLA changes as the robot acts, because the representation influences the chosen action, which changes the physical state and thus the next representation — a closed feedback loop that standard LRH formulations do not address.

## [Minimax Limits and Learnable Interfaces for Passive Image Provenance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30997) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.30997) formalizes passive image provenance as source–target verification under adversarial distribution shift, deriving an exact minimax limit: the largest robust target-acceptance gap for any image-only verifier equals the minimum total-variation distance between the target distribution and the set of attacked source distributions. It further shows that deployed public verifiers can fail before this statistical ceiling is reached, because emulable score-revealing heads enable surrogate black-box attacks. This work provides a theoretical ceiling for what any pixel-only provenance verifier can achieve, helping researchers distinguish fundamental statistical limits from implementation flaws in deployed systems. It has direct implications for AI security and content authenticity, since public CLIP-based verifiers were shown to fail under targeted pixel attacks while a ResNet-18 victim exhibited partial fake-to-real transfer. The paper shows that if a verifier can be emulated on the attack region to error ε, a surrogate black-box attack reaches target acceptance within 2ε plus optimization error of the white-box optimum; score-revealing logistic and softmax heads over public features are identifiable, and approximate score access gives stable recovery bounds. A finite-state experiment checks the minimax identity, and binary feedback with abstention reduces measured attack success, though positive empirical gap upper bounds do not establish robustness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Passive image provenance asks whether pixels alone can reveal an image's origin — a human, an aggregate AI class, or a specific generator — without embedded watermarks or metadata. Once an attacker can edit the source image before verification, provenance becomes a robustness problem under adversarial distribution shift. Minimax theory studies worst-case optimal performance, and total-variation distance measures how distinguishable two probability distributions are. Public verifiers such as CLIP-based models are often used to detect AI-generated images, but their robustness under targeted attacks is not guaranteed.

## [DynBranch Cuts Agentic LLM Latency via Speculative Subgraph Reuse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31047) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DynBranch introduces speculative subgraph reuse to hide the branch-resolution barrier in agentic LLM workflows, achieving up to 32% lower mean latency over the strongest prior system and 46-66% over a no-reuse baseline. It runs on four agentic workloads with Qwen3-32B on 4x H200 GPUs and requires no changes to agent harnesses or model execution engines. Agentic LLM applications increasingly chain multiple model calls and tool invocations, and the serialization caused by waiting for branch resolution is a major source of latency. A technique that hides this barrier without touching agent code or inference engines could be broadly adopted by serving frameworks and production agent platforms. DynBranch makes an unresolved branch addressable before it resolves through a stable coordinate, letting candidate subgraphs execute during resolution and completed results be reused across later requests; a two-level controller admits this work only when expected benefit exceeds the load price. The benefit persists across backbone families and even on a commodity Qwen3-8B/RTX 4090 deployment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic LLM workflows decide their execution paths at runtime, so downstream computation often cannot start until the model or user resolves a branch — a serialization the paper calls the branch-resolution barrier. Traditional caching cannot hide it because the key identifying a reusable result is unknown until resolution. DynBranch sits at the model-API boundary and combines speculative execution with cross-request subgraph reuse, promoted on exact match and invalidated by writes.

## [Hybrid Quantum Diffusion Model Scales to Real Medical Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31070) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.31070) introduces a scalable hybrid Quantum Diffusion Model that uses a Discrete-Time Quantum Walk executed on a real quantum device to model the forward diffusion dynamics, while a classical learning model handles the reverse denoising step. Unlike prior quantum machine learning attempts limited by small quantum hardware, this method processes real-world large grayscale, RGB, and moderate-sized 3D medical volumes, and benchmarks competitively against a classical discrete-state diffusion counterpart on three state-of-the-art image generation metrics. This work addresses the scalability bottleneck that has kept quantum machine learning confined to toy problems, showing that hybrid quantum-classical diffusion models can handle practical medical imaging data. If validated, it could open a path toward quantum-enhanced generative models for clinical tasks such as medical image synthesis, denoising, and augmentation, where large high-dimensional data is the norm. The forward process is implemented as a Discrete-Time Quantum Walk on real quantum hardware, while the backward denoising step is delegated to a classical model, making the overall pipeline hybrid rather than fully quantum. Evaluation covers grayscale, RGB, and moderate-sized 3D volumes, with comparisons to a classical diffusion model on discrete state spaces using three standard image generation metrics; as an arXiv preprint, the results have not yet undergone peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models are generative models that learn to gradually add noise to data (forward process) and then learn to reverse that noise to generate new samples (backward process). Quantum machine learning seeks to exploit quantum mechanical effects such as superposition, entanglement, and interference to improve machine learning, but current quantum devices are small and noisy, which has severely limited prior quantum approaches to image analysis. A discrete-time quantum walk is a unitary quantum process on a graph that evolves via coin and shift operations, and it serves here as the quantum counterpart to the classical random walk used in discrete-state diffusion models.

## [DepthEvidence Unifies Metric Depth Prediction and Geometric Reasoning in a 4B MLLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31103) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DepthEvidence introduces a 4B multimodal language model that uses its own dense metric depth predictions as object-grounded evidence for language generation, converting predicted depths and decoder features into object-aligned continuous geometry tokens anchored to object identifiers. The paper also presents Depth-VQA, a new benchmark evaluating object-depth queries, relative comparisons, and decisions that combine spatial and numerical constraints. Spatial reasoning with metric constraints is a known weakness of vision-language models, and this work shows that a relatively small 4B model can lead evaluated methods on instance-level metric depth estimation and on both relative and metric reasoning tracks while broadly preserving general VQA performance. If validated, this could push multimodal models toward more physically grounded understanding for robotics, embodied AI, and measurement-based question answering. The architecture uses a camera-conditioned decoder that predicts full-resolution metric depth from multi-scale visual features with high-resolution RGB refinement, and geometric supervision keeps metric information recoverable before and after language-context interaction. Across nine datasets, DepthEvidence achieves the highest average dense delta-1 among evaluated methods, competitive with specialized estimators, though it remains an arXiv preprint without peer review or community validation.

**Background**: Metric depth prediction estimates the actual distance from the camera to each pixel, unlike relative or affine-invariant depth that only preserves ordering. Depth-VQA is a visual question answering benchmark in this space, testing whether models can answer object-depth queries and comparisons rather than just describe scenes.

## [Bayesian Optimization via Fisher Information Geometry and Trust Regions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31107) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces an information-geometric view of Bayesian optimization by pulling back the Fisher information metric through the surrogate posterior map, yielding a local sensitivity tensor on the input space and an upper bound on the gradient of reparameterizable acquisition functions. Building on this, the authors propose FITR, a trust-region BO method that replaces lengthscale-based scaling with local pullback-Fisher weights. This framework explains the vanishing-gradient behavior observed in high-dimensional BO and offers a unified interpretation of heuristics such as RAASP and dimension-scaled lengthscales, potentially guiding more principled algorithm design. FITR's independence from explicit kernel lengthscales also broadens its applicability to non-isotropic surrogates. On GP benchmarks with a squared-exponential kernel, FITR shows competitive performance, but gains are more task-dependent when generalized to non-isotropic surrogates. The method is not restricted to GP kernels with explicit lengthscales, which is a notable departure from standard trust-region BO approaches.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Bayesian optimization is a sample-efficient technique for optimizing expensive black-box functions, typically using a Gaussian process surrogate and an acquisition function to decide where to sample next. In high-dimensional settings, gradients of acquisition functions can vanish, making optimization difficult; trust-region methods like TuRBO address this by restricting search to local regions. Information geometry studies statistical manifolds using the Fisher information metric, which measures local sensitivity of probability distributions, and RAASP is a random axis-aligned subspace perturbation heuristic used in high-dimensional BO.

## [Discrete Neural Insertion Sort Fixes Shortcut Learning in NAR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31114) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper analyzes the CLRS30 baseline for neural algorithmic reasoning on insertion sort, showing that its hint objective is weakly optimized and that intermediate representations can be decoded into sorted sequences before the reference execution terminates, indicating shortcut learning. The authors introduce Discrete Neural Insertion Sort, which represents the sequence as a chain, separates scalar exchanges from control-state transitions, and projects node representations back to discrete states after every processor step, achieving 100% sorted-sequence accuracy on lengths 64 and 128 when trained only on length 16. This work matters because it exposes a fundamental flaw in how neural algorithmic reasoning models are evaluated: correct outputs do not guarantee faithful algorithm execution, which undermines trust in these models for tasks requiring step-by-step reasoning. The proposed discrete approach demonstrates that strong length generalization is possible, offering a path toward more interpretable and reliable neural reasoners relevant to AI/ML researchers working on generalization and algorithmic learning. An ablation shows that discretization and graph structure alone are insufficient: without additional supervision of the global inner-loop state, the model fails even at the training length of 16. This highlights that problem-specific inductive bias is required to learn a faithful algorithmic execution, and that the CLRS30 baseline's hint accuracy remains low despite correct final outputs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Neural algorithmic reasoning (NAR) aims to train neural networks to execute known algorithms and generalize beyond the input sizes seen during training, often evaluated on the CLRS30 benchmark that includes over 30 algorithms. Shortcut learning occurs when a model achieves correct outputs through spurious intermediate representations rather than following the intended algorithmic steps, which is a key concern for generalization and interpretability. Insertion sort is a simple sorting algorithm used here as a testbed to study whether models truly learn the step-by-step procedure.

## [JevAdvBench: First Adversarial Benchmark for RLCD Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31142) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces JevAdvBench, described as the first adversarial benchmark for reinforcement learning for calibrated decisions (RLCD) models, featuring 812 typed questions across 66 scenarios and a black-box attack suite of 9,744 single-edit variants. On jev-1.13.0, a single unverified opinion appended to the state flips 12.1% of decisions and pushes 38% of confident answers below the 0.8 confidence threshold that routes them to human review. RLCD models like Jev return typed values that software consumes directly without human review, so unmeasured adversarial fragility could silently corrupt automated decisions at scale. This work exposes a concrete robustness gap and offers a reusable evaluation methodology, likely spurring further AI safety and adversarial ML research on typed, non-generative models. The core methodological insight is to score each attacked decision against the model's own clean decision rather than against labels, and to read it against the change caused by an identical re-run, since identical requests can return different answers and most labels come from the model itself. Rewording stays within 1.2 percentage points of the re-run baseline and out-of-schema fields never reach the model, but an appended unverified opinion (12.1%) is statistically tied with the strongest injected command (10.1%).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: RLCD stands for Reinforcement Learning for Calibrated Decisions, the training method TypeSafe AI says it used for Jev, a proprietary model released in limited early access on 15 September 2026 alongside a $40 million seed round led by DCVC. Unlike a large language model, Jev generates no natural-language text; it answers a typed question about an input (the state) with a probability, a choice, or a score, and software acts on that answer without a person reading it. Adversarial benchmarks traditionally score what a model generates or executes, which does not fit a typed model that returns a well-formed answer even when manipulated.

## [Practitioner Interviews Reveal Six New Themes in AI Data Quality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31191) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.31191) reports interviews with 16 practitioners from nine organizations, analyzed via reflexive thematic analysis, yielding six themes on how data quality is redefined in AI-driven systems. These themes include traceability shifting from modular debugging to attributing model behavior, circularity when models assess data quality, agent context and memory becoming data objects, authenticity concerns from synthetic and pseudo-labeled data, lawfulness as a training-data gate, and representativeness judged by safe-behavior coverage. This empirical study provides practitioner-grounded evidence that data quality in AI systems is no longer just about storage and validation, but is entangled with model behavior, evaluation, and legal compliance. It matters for ML engineers, data governance teams, and organizations building foundation models, as it highlights how reduced trust in data and AI outcomes arises from these interconnected challenges. The study synthesizes findings through a conceptual framing called lifecycle assurance, focused on producing evidence that data can support a specific AI claim when its influence may be embedded in model behavior, model-based judgments, or agent actions. It also interprets five recurring conditions that help explain reduced trust in data and AI outcomes, though the paper is qualitative and based on a limited sample of 16 practitioners.

**Background**: Data quality research has traditionally treated data as an input that is stored, processed, and validated. In AI-driven software-intensive systems, especially those built on foundation models—large models trained on vast datasets and adaptable to many tasks—data also shapes model behavior, evaluation, and lawful use. Reflexive thematic analysis is a qualitative research method for identifying and interpreting patterns of meaning in interview transcripts, and it was used here to develop the six themes.

## [Acoustic-to-Text KV Compression Cuts Full-Duplex Speech Model Memory by 64.6%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31224) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.31224) proposes acoustic-to-text KV compression, which uses a transcription side channel to convert older acoustic key-value states into compact textual memory during 'listening-time slack' — the interval when the model finishes an audio unit before the next arrives. Implemented on MiniCPM-o 4.5, the method reduces peak streaming KV-cache size by 64.6% on ten-minute LongSpeech sessions while also improving transcription, temporal question answering, and summarization, with Full-Duplex-Bench showing comparable pause-handling, turn-taking, and interruption performance. Full-duplex speech models continuously accumulate acoustic KV states, making long-running conversations memory-intensive and expensive to serve. This work shows that idle listening time can be repurposed to compress memory without degrading real-time interaction, which could make long-session voice assistants more practical and cheaper to deploy. The transcription side channel is trained with LoRA using cross-entropy on transcription segments, and knowledge distillation on the original model's token-level output distributions at native prediction positions preserves listening and speaking behavior. When the cache exceeds a target budget, older acoustic states are evicted while transcripts and recent acoustic context remain, though the approach is evaluated on a single MiniCPM-o 4.5 implementation and ten-minute sessions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Full-duplex spoken language models listen and speak simultaneously, enabling natural turn-taking, overlapping speech, and interruption handling in real time. Like other autoregressive transformers, they rely on a key-value (KV) cache that stores past states to avoid recomputation, but in continuous audio streams this cache grows without bound. LoRA (low-rank adaptation) is a lightweight fine-tuning technique that adds small low-rank matrices to a frozen model, while knowledge distillation trains a new component to match the output distribution of an existing model.

The paper introduces DeformView, a wide-baseline multi-view dataset with pixel-level annotations of geometric inconsistencies, and proposes DEFECt3R, a lightweight learning-based classifier that localizes such inconsistencies at the pixel level. Experiments show that existing multi-view consistency-scoring methods developed for novel view synthesis evaluation transfer poorly to this forensic task, while DEFECt3R substantially reduces false positives. This work opens a new direction for multimedia forensics by showing that multi-view geometric consistency is a promising yet underexplored signal for detecting manipulated or generated images. It provides a benchmark and baseline that could spur further research into image forgery detection across wide-baseline views. DEFECt3R uses cross-view feature relationships and learns from explicit supervision, including hard negatives from geometrically consistent yet deformed views. Ablation experiments show that both feature representations and correspondence quality contribute to localization performance, and code and dataset are available at the provided GitHub link.

## [Agentic Limit Order Books Show Phase Transitions and Non-Classical Market Impact](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31260) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.31260v1) studies Limit Order Books populated exclusively by autonomous reinforcement-learning agents, showing that these agentic LOBs exhibit distinct phase boundaries separating orderly price discovery from hyper-volatile cascade states, governed by critical thresholds in agent count and observable market depth. It further demonstrates that market impact under agentic liquidity provision deviates from the classical square-root law, exhibiting dissipative, balanced, and non-dissipative regimes driven by non-linear feedback loops. This work bridges multi-agent reinforcement learning and market microstructure, suggesting that as AI agents increasingly participate in real markets, classical assumptions such as the square-root impact law may no longer hold. The findings could influence both the design of trading algorithms and regulatory thinking about systemic risk in AI-driven markets. The paper formalizes agent interactions within a microscopic order-matching engine and identifies critical thresholds in agent number and market depth that trigger regime shifts; however, it is a preprint without peer review, and the results are based on simulation rather than empirical market data.

Multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) studies multiple learning agents that coexist in a shared environment, each pursuing its own reward, producing complex group dynamics. The square-root law of market impact is an empirically established regularity stating that the price impact of a trade scales roughly with the square root of its size, arising from liquidity, strategic order-splitting, and market efficiency.

## [Softmax Reparameterization Boosts Output-Head Quantization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31291) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces softmax reparameterization, a post-training method that shifts each output-head row by a scalar multiple of the vocabulary-row mean and selects the coefficient via validation KL. Across seven output heads and three quantizers, it cuts test KL by up to 93% on XGLM under RTN and 73–77% on Phi, BLOOM, and BLOOMZ under activation-weighted MSE, with no decoder retraining. Output heads dominate inference cost in small language models with large vocabularies, so improving their quantization accuracy without retraining could make low-bit deployment cheaper and more reliable. The reported 10.8% batch-one latency reduction on a packed W4 Phi head suggests practical speedups for edge and on-device LLM serving. For linear-softmax heads the shifts preserve full-precision predictions exactly, and a rank-one correction extends the construction to nonlinear logit paths; for shift-compatible heads the shift adds no inference operation. Gains are largest where baseline quantization heavily distorts predictions, while heads with low baseline error change little, and frozen WikiText-selected coefficients transfer without retuning to C4 and OpenWebMath.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Post-training quantization (PTQ) compresses an already-trained model's weights to lower precision, such as 4-bit (W4) or 2-bit (W2), without retraining, reducing memory and compute for inference. In transformer language models, the output head projects hidden states to vocabulary-sized logits, so a large vocabulary makes this layer a major cost. Quantization error in the head can distort the softmax distribution and degrade generation quality, which this work aims to mitigate by choosing a functionally equivalent head before quantization.

## [EffectMatch Validates Persistent Outcomes in LLM Agent Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31301) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.31301) introduces EffectMatch, a runtime that collects persistent changes made within a controlled execution boundary and compares them against what the application approved for the current state and execution. In comparative evaluation on 206 public business tasks, EffectMatch preserved all clean executions and prevented all tested incorrect commits, with six 20-run ablations and 80 task-topology cases confirming that removing each mechanism causes failures. This addresses a novel and important safety gap in LLM agent workflows: an approved database update may succeed yet leave an unapproved notification, and without checking the persistent result before continuation, that unapproved outcome can be silently accepted and propagated to later steps. The approach is likely to influence future work on runtime verification and agent governance as agents increasingly act on real software systems. EffectMatch governs commit and dependent execution by comparing persistent changes against application approval, rather than merely approving an action or recording its aftermath. The evaluation included six 20-run ablations that exposed the failure caused by each removed mechanism, plus 80 task-topology cases that preserved truthful handoffs and blocked invalid continuation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Large language model agents are moving beyond text generation into active participants in software workflows, changing databases and online services. Unlike base LLMs that only generate outputs from provided context, agents can maintain persistent state and act on external systems when an application supplies memory and tools. This creates a verification challenge: execution can produce persistent effects beyond the requested change, and current safeguards can approve an action or record its aftermath but do not check the persistent result before continuation.

## [AgentXploit: Two-Role Autonomous Red-Teaming for AI Agent Security](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31318) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AgentXploit introduces a two-role auditing system that separates repository-level attack-path discovery (Analyzer Agent) from runtime exploitation (Exploiter Agent), achieving 59.3% end-to-end attack success versus 38.4% for Codex across three runs. The authors also release AgentXploit-Bench, containing 72 reproducible vulnerabilities across 12 open-source AI-agent systems and frameworks. As AI agents gain the ability to modify files, call APIs, and execute code, security auditing before deployment becomes critical, and this work shows that repository discovery and runtime exploitation are distinct challenges that benefit from specialized agents. The benchmark and methodology could become a standard for pre-deployment security evaluation of agentic systems. Under a token-budget-matched comparison, Codex reaches 46.3%, still below AgentXploit's 59.3%, and on AgentDojo where injection points are provided, the Exploiter Agent achieves 79.2% attack success versus 52.7% for AgentVigil. The system operates under authorized white-box conditions where the auditor has repository access and a controlled runtime, but attacks must still pass through the task-defined attacker interface and be confirmed by an external verifier.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents combine large language models with external tools and data, which introduces security risks such as path traversal and command injection vulnerabilities in the surrounding software, as well as adversarial content that can alter an agent's tool use. Path traversal exploits insufficient validation of user-supplied file names to access unauthorized files, while command injection executes arbitrary OS commands through unsanitized input. Pre-deployment auditing aims to find such flaws before an agent is exposed to real users.

## [DyMD Distillation Preserves Interaction Dynamics in Few-Step Video World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31349) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose DyMD, a Distribution Matching Distillation (DMD) framework that adapts teacher supervision and critic fitting to the evolving student, distilling a 14B teacher into a four-step 1.3B student without auxiliary inference modules. On embodied-video benchmarks, the student improves R-Bench task adherence by 9.6 percentage points and PAI-Bench-G Domain score by 5.1 points over Base DMD, and achieves 34% mean success across two WorldArena action-planning tasks versus 16% for Base DMD. Few-step video world models are critical for interactive embodied AI, where many-step diffusion sampling is too slow for real-time downstream use. By preserving robot-object interaction dynamics rather than only visual quality, DyMD could make distilled video world models more reliable as backbones for action planning and embodied learning. The method identifies two DMD failure modes: weak re-noising keeps the teacher posterior concentrated near motion-deficient rollouts, while stronger-motion rollouts incur larger fake-score fitting errors. DyMD addresses these with temporal affinity-conditioned re-noise sampling and dynamics-guided fake-score tracking, using a noise-conditioned predictor to upweight predicted-hard rollouts in the critic loss.

**Background**: Diffusion models generate video by iteratively denoising latents over many sampling steps, which is expensive for interactive applications. Distribution Matching Distillation (DMD) compresses a large pre-trained teacher diffusion model into a smaller student that can generate in one or a few steps by matching their output distributions. Video world models are generative models that predict future frames of an environment, and embodied AI systems use them to plan actions for robots.

A new arXiv paper (2609.31358v1) presents an IEEE 11073 SDC-to-MCP gateway that exposes medical device metrics, alarms, context references, and semantic metadata as read-only resources, while representing selected action affordances as policy-validated dry-run tools. The prototype guarantees a "no-execution" property: agent-facing requests dispatch no actual SDC device operation, and this boundary held across resource, proposal, and authorization paths in simulated fault and lifecycle experiments. As LLM agents increasingly connect to external tools via MCP, regulated domains like healthcare need deterministic guarantees that an agent cannot directly actuate a medical device. This work offers a concrete architectural pattern for safely grounding agent reasoning in live device state without granting execution authority, which is valuable for researchers and practitioners deploying AI in clinical or other safety-critical environments. The Python prototype supports simulated fault and lifecycle experiments, a software-reference protocol path spanning independent Java and Python implementations, deterministic baselines, representation ablations, and multi-model agent evaluation. Results show that explicit semantic metadata improved conformity to required metric identifiers in structured alarm outputs compared with a generic representation, though retained structured-output failures highlight a gap between plausible narrative answers and task-compliant machine-readable results.

## [H2S: Compress-Then-Reason Method Boosts Long-Context LLM Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31382) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes Highlight-Then-Summarize (H2S), a compress-then-reason paradigm that first identifies question-relevant evidence and then integrates it into a compact, question-conditioned summary before producing the final answer. It introduces H2S-Dataset with 6,647 examples from 11 benchmark families (average context length 43.9K tokens) and H2S-RL, which provides process-level rewards for evidence selection and summary construction, achieving 32.60 average score on H2S-Bench and outperforming Qwen3.8-27B by 10.17 points. This work addresses the core challenge of long-context understanding where task-relevant evidence is sparse and scattered amid irrelevant content, showing that explicitly selecting and integrating evidence improves reasoning while enabling more compact generation. The approach could influence how future LLMs handle lengthy documents, conversations, and code, and the released dataset and benchmark may spur further research in evidence-grounded long-context reasoning. Under a shared 128K input and 4K output budget, H2S-14B achieves the highest Evidence-Summary Quality score and retains 97.1% of its 16K-budget performance with only a 4K output budget. The abstract is truncated and no community discussion is provided, so the full technical details and broader impact remain to be assessed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Long-context understanding refers to the ability of large language models (LLMs) to process and reason over information spread across long sequences of text, such as lengthy documents, conversations, or code. A key difficulty is that only a small portion of the input is relevant to a given question, while most content is redundant or irrelevant, making it hard for models to locate and use the right evidence. Prior approaches often either truncate the context or rely on retrieval, but H2S instead compresses the evidence into a question-conditioned summary before reasoning, and uses reinforcement learning with process-level rewards to train this behavior.

## [ActKV: Action-Guided KV Cache Compression for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31395) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced ActKV, the first KV cache compression framework designed specifically for agentic LLM inference, which prioritizes cache entries critical to action generation rather than overall output quality. On long-trace tasks, ActKV retains an average of 98.53% of FullKV's accuracy using only 25.98% of its peak KV cache memory, while achieving 3.97x and 3.58x FullKV's token and task throughput. Agentic LLM inference accumulates long KV caches across iterative observation-reasoning-action loops, creating severe memory overhead and throughput bottlenecks in serving systems. By targeting action-critical entries, ActKV offers a practical path to more efficient and scalable LLM agent deployment, which could benefit anyone running multi-step agent workloads in production. ActKV combines three components: action-oriented KV cache eviction that exploits stable action access patterns, confidence-driven adaptive budget allocation that uses the LLM's intrinsic confidence to adjust memory budgets, and page-aware compression management that standardizes compression into three primitives with customized kernels. The page-aware design is notable because it integrates with paged memory systems like PagedAttention, enabling real throughput gains rather than theoretical ones.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: In transformer-based LLMs, the KV cache stores key and value tensors from previous tokens so the model does not recompute them at each generation step, but this cache grows linearly with sequence length and consumes large amounts of GPU memory. LLM agents repeatedly invoke the model in observation-reasoning-action loops, so their KV caches grow far longer than in single-turn question answering. Existing compression methods like eviction and quantization mainly optimize overall output quality, ignoring that in agent settings only a few actions actually drive task progress. PagedAttention, inspired by OS virtual memory, allocates KV cache non-contiguously to reduce memory waste, and ActKV builds on such paged memory systems.

## [VLMs Read Sharp Text but Miss Soft Shaded Text in Layered Typography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31403) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study introduces DecoyBench, a dataset of 300 images created with the Decoy Font method, in which sharp contour text is superimposed on soft shading text. Testing six recent closed-source vision-language models from three model families under naive and guided prompting at 512x512 and 64x64 resolutions, the authors found that models read the contour layer with near-human accuracy at high resolution but almost never fully extracted the shading layer, while human participants read both layers accurately. This finding exposes a consistent behavioral blind spot in vision-language models that goes beyond known typographic attacks, with direct implications for OCR reliability, document understanding, and multimodal AI robustness. It suggests that current VLMs rely heavily on high spatial-frequency cues and may silently miss information that humans perceive effortlessly, which matters for security-sensitive and accessibility-critical applications. The DecoyBench dataset contains 300 images, and the study evaluated six closed-source models from three families under two prompting conditions and two resolutions. At low resolution (64x64), neither models nor humans could read the contour text, but the shading text was extracted with high accuracy, revealing a spatial-frequency-dependent inversion of readability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) combine visual encoders with large language models to perform tasks such as optical character recognition (OCR) and image question answering. Typographic attacks are a known vulnerability in which misleading text rendered into an image can manipulate a model's output. The Decoy Font method creates images with two legible typographic layers distinguished by spatial frequency: a high-frequency sharp contour layer and a low-frequency soft shading layer.

## [Implicit Neural Representation Boosts Hyperspectral Video Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31435) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.31435) proposes extending an existing RGB video compression model with implicit neural representation (INR) for hyperspectral video, achieving +4.99 dB BD-PSNR and -88.88% BD-rate compared to traditional frame-by-frame hyperspectral image compression methods. It also improves downstream object tracking AUC by up to 23.42% and distance precision by up to 35.56% on the HOT2026 dataset in low-data regimes. Hyperspectral video is becoming more available via snapshot cameras, but compression remains in early stages; this work shows INR can dramatically reduce bitrate while preserving or even improving downstream task performance, which could enable practical storage and transmission of large hyperspectral video datasets for remote sensing and tracking applications. The method is a novel extension of an existing RGB video compression model, and evaluation uses Bjøntegaard Delta metrics (BD-PSNR and BD-rate) against frame-by-frame hyperspectral image compression; tracking improvements are measured against PCA- and JPEG2000-based compression in low data regimes on the HOT2026 dataset.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Implicit neural representations (INRs), also called neural fields, are neural networks that map continuous inputs like spatial coordinates and time to continuous outputs, parameterizing signals directly as network weights rather than discrete pixels or frames. Hyperspectral video captures many spectral bands per pixel over time, producing very large data volumes that are challenging to compress with traditional image/video codecs. Bjøntegaard Delta (BD) metrics are standard measures for comparing rate-distortion performance between codecs, where BD-PSNR indicates average PSNR improvement at equal bitrate and BD-rate indicates average bitrate savings at equal quality.

## [Medical VLM Post-Training Audit: Accuracy Gains Mask Lost Visual Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31450) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A controlled study on Qwen2.5-VL-3B for PMC-VQA medical visual question answering compared language-model-only LoRA supervised fine-tuning (SFT), broader multimodal adaptation, standard answer-only GRPO, and a counterfactual evidence objective. Language-model LoRA SFT changed correct-image accuracy by only +1.10 percentage points (95% paired bootstrap CI: -0.85 to +3.05), while visual-benefit events dropped by 2.40 points and image sensitivity fell by 5.60 points, with 155 visual-benefit events acquired and 203 lost. The findings show that standard answer-accuracy metrics can hide degraded visual grounding, meaning medical VLM post-training may be rewarded for language priors rather than genuine image use. This matters for medical AI researchers and practitioners who rely on accuracy-only benchmarks to certify multimodal clinical models. Broader multimodal adaptation yielded lower correct-image accuracy than language-model-only LoRA SFT, and standard GRPO produced mixed-reward groups with uncertain clean-test accuracy change. A generation audit also found that canonical option scores can follow a different token path from generated answers, and the evidence objective's gains over GRPO remained inconsistent on validation data at matched training doses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) such as Qwen2.5-VL-3B process images and text jointly, and post-training adapts them to tasks like medical visual question answering (VQA). LoRA is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method that freezes pretrained weights and injects small trainable low-rank matrices, while GRPO is a reinforcement-learning algorithm that samples groups of responses per prompt and uses the group's mean reward as a baseline. This audit asks whether such post-training actually makes models rely more on images, or merely improves answer accuracy through language shortcuts.

## [First Federated Learning Framework for Infant Movement Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31463) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present what they describe as the first federated learning framework for automated infant movement analysis and General Movement Assessment using video-derived skeletal motion data. They also propose Uncertainty-Aware Federated Averaging (UA-FedAvg), which uses Monte Carlo Dropout predictive entropy to weight client contributions during aggregation, and evaluate it on fidgety movement classification in a three-client setting. This work enables multi-institutional collaboration on early detection of neurodevelopmental disorders without centralizing sensitive infant video data, addressing real privacy, governance, and data-sharing barriers in clinical settings. It also shows that uncertainty-aware aggregation can outperform conventional FedAvg, suggesting a practical path for privacy-preserving healthcare AI. The framework was evaluated on fidgety movement classification using a cross-subject protocol with three federated clients; federated training substantially improved performance over independently trained local models and approached centralized training. UA-FedAvg and a variant incorporating validation loss generally outperformed conventional FedAvg across the evaluated data-split configurations, though the abstract is truncated and does not report specific accuracy numbers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: General Movement Assessment (GMA) is a clinical method, developed by Heinz Prechtl in the 1990s, for evaluating spontaneous movements in infants under about four months of age; the presence or absence of 'fidgety movements' around three months post-term is a strong predictor of cerebral palsy. Deep learning can now automate GMA from video-derived skeletal representations, but conventional centralized training requires pooling data from multiple hospitals, which is often blocked by privacy and governance rules. Federated learning instead lets multiple clients collaboratively train a shared model while keeping their data decentralized, which is especially valuable when client data is heterogeneous.

## [First Systematic Evaluation of LLM Cultural Awareness in Haitian Creole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31506) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.31506) presents the first systematic evaluation of cultural awareness in large language models for Haitian Creole, using a native-speaker-curated benchmark of culturally salient prompts in a text infilling setting. It assesses four dimensions—specificity, bias, diversity, and variation—and finds a clear gap versus higher-resource French, with Haitian performance more uneven across domains and more affected by French linguistic interference. This work highlights how LLMs systematically underperform and encode stereotypes for severely under-resourced languages spoken by millions, extending fairness and cultural-alignment concerns beyond task accuracy. It provides an open benchmark and evaluation framework that could guide more culturally faithful multilingual model development. The benchmark uses culturally salient prompts curated by native speakers in a text infilling setup, and story generation reveals recurring portrayals of Haitian characters through hardship and resilience, showing that even positive characterizations can encode stereotypical narratives. The code, benchmark, and evaluation framework are publicly available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are trained predominantly on high-resource languages such as English and French, leaving low-resource languages like Haitian Creole—spoken by millions but scarce in digital text—poorly served. Cultural awareness evaluation goes beyond grammar and task accuracy to test whether models reflect a community's norms, values, and lived experience. Text infilling, the task of filling in masked spans, is a common way to probe a model's implicit knowledge.

<a id="item-171"></a>
## [ClearGS: Reliability-Aware Gaussian Splatting from Handheld Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31509) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ClearGS introduces a reliability-aware framework for 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) from handheld videos, replacing binary frame selection with Reliability-aware View Allocation (RVA) that assigns graded raw-supervision weights based on appearance reliability, degradation risk, and geometric utility, while weakly reactivating useful suppressed frames. It further adds Render-Guided In-Video Restoration (RIVR), which uses the current 3DGS render as a pose-aligned structural candidate and a frozen no-reference restoration expert to restore raw observations without any clean reference, then selects among render, restored, and high-frequency fused candidates via no-reference perceptual scores, followed by Full-Trajectory Repair Consolidation. Handheld video is the most common and cheapest way to capture scenes for 3D reconstruction, but uneven viewpoint coverage and mixed frame quality (blur, distortion) routinely break existing 3DGS pipelines. By making frame weighting graded and restoring degraded frames without paired sharp supervision or matched clean references, ClearGS could make high-quality novel view synthesis practical for casual consumer capture, benefiting AR/VR content creation, robotics mapping, and digital twins. The method reports state-of-the-art overall performance on the GS2E and GSOTM benchmarks, with consistent CLIP-IQA and MUSIQ gains and LPIPS reductions in most degradation settings, all without paired sharp supervision or matched clean references. The pipeline combines three components: graded reliability-aware view allocation, render-guided no-reference in-video restoration with candidate selection, and full-trajectory repair consolidation that revisits accepted repairs to preserve early-introduced details.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) is a volume rendering technique revived in 2023 by a research group at Inria that represents a scene as many 3D Gaussians and enables real-time radiance field rendering, converting multiple images into a 3D representation from which novel views can be synthesized. Novel view synthesis aims to generate photorealistic images from viewpoints not present in the input captures, and no-reference image quality assessment measures image quality without access to the original undistorted image, which is essential when no clean reference exists. ClearGS targets the practical setting where input is a handheld video with uneven coverage and degraded frames rather than carefully captured, uniformly sharp images.

## [Checkability Boundary Decides When Local LLMs Can Automate Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31540) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.31540) introduces 'checkability' as a criterion for deciding which network-automation tasks are safe to run on local small language models, and instantiates it in Touchstone, a local-first pipeline using seven off-the-shelf SLMs of 1-8B parameters. Touchstone reaches 98.6% and 93.8% end-to-end accuracy on conflict detection and intent translation while escalating only 16% and 17% of inputs to a frontier LLM. The work offers a concrete deployment rule for privacy-preserving and cost-effective AI: keep inference local when task semantics support precise, low-cost checks, and escalate the rest. This matters for network operators who cannot export sensitive production configurations, topologies, and logs to third-party frontier LLMs. A task is deemed checkable when it exposes a cheap, deterministic 'intrinsic check' that rejects outputs violating a necessary correctness condition. On TeleQnA, a knowledge-only control with no task-specific intrinsic checks, Touchstone fails to match the frontier baseline, showing the approach depends on task structure rather than model capability alone.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Frontier LLMs are typically accessed as third-party cloud services, so sending network-automation inputs to them can leak sensitive artifacts such as production configurations, topologies, and logs. Small language models (SLMs) of roughly 1-8B parameters can run locally to avoid this data egress, but their outputs are often too error-prone for direct use. This paper proposes deciding between local and remote inference based on whether a task's output can be cheaply and deterministically verified.

## [Query-Efficient Algorithms Align Black-Box Generative AI Output Distributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31607) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.31607) develops query-efficient algorithms that post-process the outputs of black-box generative AI models so that the joint distribution of a chosen attribute matches a user-specified target. The authors provide both exact and approximate alignment algorithms, prove their optimality as the number of requested outputs m grows to infinity, and validate them on text-to-image generation and geocoded persona generation tasks. Controlling the statistical distribution of attributes such as gender, race, or age in generative outputs is central to fairness and to producing representative synthetic data, yet most existing methods rely on prompting or retraining. This work shows that black-box post-processing can complement prompting-based interventions while minimizing the number of queries, making distribution alignment practical for users who can only access a model through its API. The algorithms target the practically important black-box access setting, where a user can only repeatedly query the generator, and they aim to return m ≥ 1 outputs whose joint attribute distribution is as close as possible to the target. The theoretical analysis establishes optimality in the limit as m → ∞, and experiments indicate that the post-processing approach improves statistical attribute alignment on top of prompting-based methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Black-box AI refers to systems whose internal workings are hidden from users, who can only observe inputs and outputs; many commercial generative models are accessed this way through APIs. Generative models are computational models that learn to produce new data resembling a training distribution, and aligning their outputs with human requirements—such as fairness or representativeness—is an active research area. Distribution alignment typically means steering the statistical properties of generated samples toward a desired target distribution, which is challenging when the model cannot be modified internally.

## [SkillFlow: Scalable Multi-Stage Retrieval for Agent Skills](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06188) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers released SkillFlow, the first open multi-stage retrieval system for agent skill discovery, framing skill acquisition as an information retrieval problem over a corpus of roughly 35,000 community-contributed SKILL.md definitions indexed from GitHub. The pipeline narrows candidates through four stages—dense retrieval, two rounds of cross-encoder reranking, and LLM-based selection—and on SkillsBench raises Pass@1 from 9.2% to 16.4% (+78.3%, p_adj = 3.64e-2), reaching 84.1% of the oracle ceiling. As community-driven skill repositories grow, agents that load too many irrelevant skills suffer degraded performance, so selective retrieval becomes critical infrastructure for skill-augmented agents. SkillFlow shows that framing skill acquisition as an information retrieval task is effective, while also revealing that real-world gains depend on corpus coverage and skill quality rather than retrieval alone. On Terminal-Bench, which provides only 89 tasks and no matched skills, agents readily used the retrieved skills (70.1% use rate) yet showed no performance gain, indicating that retrieval alone is insufficient when the corpus lacks high-quality, executable skills for the target domain. The paper emphasizes that practical impact hinges on the density of runnable code and bundled artifacts in the corpus.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents can extend their capabilities at inference time by loading reusable skills into context, but equipping an agent with too many skills, especially irrelevant ones, degrades performance. Information retrieval is the task of identifying and retrieving relevant resources from a large collection, and dense retrieval uses learned embeddings to match queries with documents, while cross-encoder reranking rescores retrieved candidates by jointly encoding the query and each passage for higher precision. SkillFlow applies this classic multi-stage retrieval recipe to the new problem of selecting agent skills from a large community-contributed library.

## [NarrativeAttack jailbreaks unified multimodal models via three-act visual stories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.26473) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced NarrativeAttack, a semantic-preserving visual narrative jailbreak framework that uses a three-act structure in which the unified multimodal model's own image generator produces setup and resolution images, hiding the malicious event as a concealed climax. Experiments show it achieves up to 88.25% attack success rate on Gemini-2.5-Flash, consistently surpassing prior jailbreak approaches. This exposes an underdeveloped safety vulnerability in unified multimodal models, which increasingly combine visual understanding and image generation in a single interactive workflow, making generated visual content available as later reasoning context. It highlights the urgent need for safety alignment that accounts for cross-turn visual grounding rather than only text rewriting or isolated visual prompts. The attack concludes with an image-based guessing game that embeds the original malicious query among benign candidates, compelling the model to select and answer the most relevant one based on the established narrative context, and a dynamic difficulty mechanism further enhances attack stability. The abstract lacks detailed ablation results and no community discussion is provided to gauge impact.

Jailbreaking refers to adversarial attacks that bypass a model's safety guardrails to elicit harmful responses. A proof-of-concept study (arXiv:2512.05836) developed an end-to-end LLM pipeline that automatically generates personalized client networks from therapy transcripts, using 8,028 annotated utterances from 77 sessions with 6 clients. The pipeline identifies clinically relevant processes, clusters them into meaningful groups, and generates connections between clusters, with evaluation generally supporting model utility. This approach could make network-driven treatment personalization scalable by eliminating the need for intensive longitudinal data like daily diaries or ecological momentary assessment, which are often impractical to collect. If validated, it could help therapists with case conceptualization and treatment planning, though more research is needed to confirm whether these networks improve treatment outcomes. The study annotated 8,028 utterances from 77 therapy transcripts (N=6), using binary classification for clinically relevant processes and multi-label classification for dimensions, followed by a two-step clustering and label generation method. Interrater agreement on model performance metrics ranged from poor to substantial, and qualitative examination showed consistency with original study data, but the small sample size limits generalizability.

<a id="item-178"></a>
## [Stepwise Marginal Information Gain: Annotation-Free Process Rewards for LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01034) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2602.01034v2) introduces Stepwise Marginal Information Gain (MIG), an intrinsic process reward computed directly from the policy itself, which measures how each structured reasoning prefix changes the length-normalized, teacher-forced log-likelihood of the reference answer. Combined with a monotonic historical watermark, outcome and format rewards, and a gated self-distillation objective, the method beats outcome-only GRPO in every single-run comparison across eight benchmarks, improving average accuracy by up to 4.8 points and gaining 12.6 points on MathVerse. Process reward models typically require expensive step-level annotations, auxiliary models, or inference-time search, so a policy-derived, annotation-free stepwise credit signal could substantially lower the cost of RL-based reasoning training for both LLMs and VLMs. The reported gains, including exceeding an external PRM-BoN@16 baseline by 12.9 points at 7B on vision-language transfer without inference-time reranking, suggest this approach may become a practical alternative to explicit process reward modeling. MIG uses a monotonic historical watermark that rewards only new likelihood maxima, preventing duplicate credit after sub-record detours, and for VLMs adds a real-versus-blank likelihood gate that down-weights rewards when answers remain predictable without the image. The gated self-distillation objective retains only structurally valid and correct trajectories, and the results are based on single-run comparisons across eight task-specific benchmarks, so broader statistical validation remains limited.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning has become a common way to improve reasoning in large language models, but the standard reward is a sparse binary signal that only says whether the final answer is correct, giving no credit to intermediate steps. Process reward models address this by scoring each step, yet they usually need human or model-generated step annotations, extra auxiliary models, or search at inference time. Teacher forcing means feeding the model the reference answer token by token and summing the log-probabilities it assigns, which is how MIG measures the likelihood of the reference answer given a reasoning prefix.

The benchmark requires agents to search for evidence and recover structured annotations including standardized function terms, expression patterns, and historical synonyms linking decades of nomenclature. ## [AMOR: Adaptive Entropy Gate for Hybrid Recurrent-Attention Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13215) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AMOR (Adaptive Metacognitive Output Router) is a post-hoc hybrid architecture that selectively invokes attention only when a recurrent backbone's output entropy exceeds a dynamic threshold derived from a running batch median and scaled standard deviation. Pretrained from scratch on FineWeb-Edu with attention active on only ~40% of positions, AMOR variants built on Mamba2 or Gated DeltaNet backbones achieve the highest eight-task common-sense reasoning average at each scale compared to pure recurrent, pure attention, and fixed-schedule hybrid models. This work shows that when attention is applied matters as much as how much, offering a simple, parameter-free alternative to uniform or fixed routing strategies for efficient sequence modeling. It could influence the design of future hybrid architectures that balance the efficiency of recurrence with the contextual recall of attention, benefiting researchers and engineers working on long-context and resource-constrained language models. The binary gate requires no learned routing parameters, and AMOR also improves retrieval performance over pure recurrent models while remaining competitive against fixed-schedule hybrids. Additionally, AMOR retains the long-context robustness of its recurrent backbones, whereas Transformer and other hybrid architectures degrade under distribution shift.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Recurrent-attention hybrids combine efficient recurrent layers (such as state-space models like Mamba2 or linear attention variants like Gated DeltaNet) with a small number of full-attention layers to capture contextual recall. Existing hybrids typically apply attention uniformly across all positions or follow a fixed schedule, even when the recurrent state alone suffices. AMOR introduces an entropy-based gate that activates attention only when the model is uncertain, measured by output entropy relative to a dynamic batch-level threshold.

## [Meta's GEARS Uses Agentic AI to Automate Large-Scale Ranking Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.18640) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Meta researchers introduced GEARS (Generative Engine for Agentic Ranking Systems), a framework that reframes ranking optimization as an autonomous discovery process within a programmable experimentation environment. GEARS uses Specialized Agent Skills to encode ranking expert knowledge into reusable reasoning capabilities, allowing operators to steer systems through high-level intent rather than manual hypothesis engineering. The paper argues that progress in large-scale ranking is increasingly bottlenecked by the engineering context constraint—translating ambiguous product intent into executable hypotheses—rather than by modeling techniques alone. If validated, GEARS could shift how industrial recommendation and search teams allocate engineering effort, moving from manual experimentation toward agent-driven policy discovery. GEARS decomposes the end-to-end ranking optimization workflow into three coordinated stages orchestrated by an LLM-based controller, and incorporates validation hooks to enforce statistical robustness and filter out brittle policies that overfit short-term signals. Experimental validation across diverse product surfaces reportedly identifies superior, near-Pareto-efficient policies while maintaining deployment stability.

## [Combee Scales Parallel Prompt Learning for Self-Improving LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04247) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers from UC Berkeley and Stanford propose Combee, a framework that scales parallel prompt learning for self-improving language model agents. Combee achieves up to 17x speedup over previous methods on AppWorld, Terminal-Bench, Formula, and FiNER while maintaining comparable or better accuracy at equivalent cost. Existing prompt learning methods like ACE and GEPA were limited to single-agent or low-parallelism settings, causing quality degradation when scaled. Combee removes this bottleneck, enabling agents to learn efficiently from large collections of parallel agentic traces, which matters as agentic workloads increasingly run many agents concurrently. Combee is plug-and-play with existing methods such as ACE and GEPA, inserting between the reflection and update stages. It leverages parallel scans, an augmented shuffle mechanism, and a dynamic batch size controller to balance quality and delay.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Prompt learning lets LLM agents acquire task-relevant knowledge from inference-time context without changing model parameters, for example by learning better system prompts from previous runs. Methods like ACE and GEPA do this sequentially, reflecting on errors and updating prompts. As agents are increasingly run in parallel and produce many traces, learning from all of them at once becomes desirable but naive parallel aggregation damages prompt quality.

## [ReasonAudio Benchmark Tests Logical Reasoning in Text-Audio Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03361) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced ReasonAudio, a new benchmark for reasoning-intensive text-audio retrieval that evaluates four abilities: negation, temporal order, sound co-occurrence, and sound duration. It includes five synthetic subtasks with 1,000 queries over 10,000 composite audio clips plus a natural subtask with 100 queries over 1,000 real-world clips, and evaluation of 11 state-of-the-art systems shows the best model, OmniEmbed-7B, achieves only 20.7 overall. Existing audio retrieval benchmarks mainly assess semantic matching, so this benchmark fills a gap by measuring logical reasoning that complex queries require, revealing that current multimodal systems are far from human-level performance. The large gap between models and humans (95.6%) highlights an important challenge for the audio and multimodal AI community and provides a resource to drive future research. In a controlled experiment designed to reduce the influence of sound-event matching, OmniEmbed-7B attains 53.8% average accuracy, compared with 70.6% for its generative backbone Qwen2.5-Omni-7B-Thinker, and 95.6% for humans. This reveals a notable performance gap between the retrieval model and its generative backbone, suggesting that embedding-based retrieval may lose reasoning ability present in the underlying generative model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Text-audio retrieval is the task of finding audio content that matches a text query, or vice versa, and is typically tackled with dual-encoder models that embed text and audio into a shared space for similarity matching. Most existing benchmarks focus on semantic matching, such as retrieving a clip that contains a described sound, rather than requiring models to reason about negation, ordering, co-occurrence, or duration. ReasonAudio extends this by constructing queries whose correct answers depend on logical reasoning over multiple sound events, and it compares specialized retrieval models against generative multimodal models and human performance.

## [Deep Transformers Can Reason Implicitly, Matching Chain-of-Thought](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.04330) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.04330v3) shows that sufficiently deep Transformers equipped with a bidirectional prefix mask can perform implicit deductive reasoning over Horn clauses at levels approaching explicit chain-of-thought (CoT) performance. The authors use counterfactual data augmentation to suppress statistical shortcuts and encourage shared reasoning primitives between direct and CoT modes, with code and models released on GitHub. This finding suggests that deep Transformer architectures can internalize compositional reasoning without generating explicit intermediate steps, potentially reducing inference cost and latency compared to CoT. It also clarifies the scaling limits of implicit reasoning, showing that CoT remains essential for depth extrapolation, which has implications for how future models are designed and trained. The study focuses on depth-bounded Transformers and Horn clauses, and finds that implicit reasoning matches CoT across graph topologies and problem widths only when models are sufficiently deep and use a bidirectional prefix mask. However, CoT is still required for depth extrapolation, meaning models cannot generalize to reasoning chains longer than those seen during training without explicit step-by-step generation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Horn clauses are a restricted form of logical formulas with at most one positive literal, widely used in logic programming and deductive databases because they support efficient inference. Chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting asks a model to generate intermediate reasoning steps in text, which improves performance on multi-step tasks but increases computation. A bidirectional prefix mask allows part of the input to attend to both past and future tokens, combining bidirectional context with autoregressive generation, and is often used in prefix language models.

Researchers introduced EBM-RL (Eye-Brain-Mouth Reinforcement Learning), a decoupled GRPO-based framework that separates observation, reasoning, and utterance generation for video-grounded role-playing dialogue. The framework integrates complementary rewards for scene-text alignment, perceptual-cognitive utility, answer faithfulness, and format consistency, and reportedly outperforms text-only role-playing baselines and larger vision-language models on an immersive role-playing benchmark, with strong zero-shot transfer to out-of-domain VideoQA tasks. This work addresses a gap in immersive applications like VR games and interactive narratives, where text-only role-playing models fail to capture scene atmosphere and evolving tension. By grounding dialogue in visual perception through a See-Think-Speak pipeline, it could improve character authenticity and atmosphere consistency in interactive entertainment and embodied AI systems. EBM-RL uses GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) as its base RL algorithm and decomposes the policy into three stages: observation, reasoning, and utterance generation, each optimized with distinct rewards. The authors also release an open-source dataset for video-grounded role-playing dialogue, though the abstract does not specify dataset size or detailed evaluation metrics.

## [SAGE: Multi-Agent LLM Framework for Expert-Level Time-Series Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05725) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced SAGE (Specialized Analyzer Group for Expert-like Detection), a multi-agent LLM framework that uses four specialized Analyzers (point, structural, seasonal, and pattern) plus a Detector and Supervisor to produce evidence-grounded anomaly diagnoses for univariate time series. Across Yahoo S5, KPI, and WSD benchmarks, SAGE achieves an average Point-F1 of 66.26, the highest among evaluated methods, and human evaluators rated its diagnostic outputs as more useful than those of compared methods. Traditional time-series anomaly detection typically outputs only scores or intervals, leaving analysts to interpret the abnormal behavior themselves; SAGE shifts the output toward explainable, evidence-grounded reports that could make anomaly detection more actionable in monitoring and operations workflows. Its strong benchmark results and positive human evaluation suggest multi-agent LLM designs can meaningfully improve both detection accuracy and diagnostic usefulness. SAGE constructs synthetic in-context references from normal-reference training segments, reducing dependence on real anomalous demonstrations, and its Detector integrates analyzer evidence into intervals, candidate types, and evidence-strength confidence scores. Controlled synthetic evaluation examines localization and type diagnosis, while component ablations support the detection contribution of specialized evidence generation.

Point-F1 is the harmonic mean of precision and recall for point-level anomaly labels, where higher values indicate better detection performance.

## [HaM-World combines Soft-Hamiltonian dynamics with selective memory for planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05951) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HaM-World introduces a structured world model that pairs a Soft-Hamiltonian latent dynamics prior with a Mamba-based selective memory, decomposing the latent state into a canonical (q,p) subspace and a context subspace c. On six DeepMind Control Suite tasks it ranks first on four and second on two, achieving the highest average AUC on the four-task core suite (117.9, 9.5% above TD-MPC2), while reducing imagined-rollout error to 45% of a strong baseline for short-to-medium horizons. This work shows that injecting physics-inspired structure (Hamiltonian geometry) and long-range selective memory into latent world models can measurably improve planning stability and out-of-distribution robustness, which matters for model-based reinforcement learning where imagined rollouts often diverge as horizons grow. The reported gains over TD-MPC2 and consistent wins under 12 OOD perturbations suggest practical benefits for robotics and control tasks with shifting dynamics. The (q,p) subspace follows an energy-derived Hamiltonian vector field augmented with learnable residual and control dynamics, while c captures semantic, dissipative, and other non-conservative factors; ablations show memory contributes the larger share of gains and Soft-Hamiltonian geometry provides smaller but consistent complementary improvements. The authors explicitly note that at longer open-loop horizons error grows faster and is overtaken between k=7 and k=20, so they do not claim uniform long-horizon stability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: World models learn a compressed latent representation of an environment's dynamics so that an agent can 'imagine' future trajectories and plan without interacting with the real environment, a core approach in model-based reinforcement learning. Hamiltonian dynamics describe physical systems via conserved energy and canonical position-momentum coordinates (q,p), while Mamba is a selective state-space model that efficiently summarizes long sequences. HaM-World combines these ideas to keep imagined rollouts stable and to adapt when the dynamics distribution shifts.
