## [Agentick: A Unified Benchmark for Sequential Decision-Making Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06869) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers released Agentick, a unified benchmark that evaluates RL, LLM, VLM, hybrid, and human agents on 37 procedurally generated tasks across six capability categories, four difficulty levels, and five observation modalities via a single Gymnasium-compatible interface. An evaluation of 27 configurations and over 90,000 episodes found that no single approach dominates: GPT-5 mini leads overall at 0.309 oracle-normalized score, PPO dominates planning and multi-agent tasks, and ASCII observations consistently outperform natural language. Agentick addresses a significant gap in fair comparison across RL, LLM, VLM, hybrid, and human agents, which previously could not be benchmarked on common ground. Its finding that no single paradigm dominates highlights substantial room for improvement and provides empirical infrastructure for driving progress toward general autonomous agents. The benchmark ships with a Coding API, oracle reference policies for all tasks, pre-built SFT datasets, a composable agent harness, and a live leaderboard. The reasoning harness multiplies LLM performance by 3-10x, and the design also serves as a training ground for RL post-training of foundation models in truly sequential environments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Sequential decision-making is the core problem in reinforcement learning, where an agent observes a state, takes an action, and receives feedback over time. Gymnasium is a widely used standard API for RL environments, and SFT (supervised fine-tuning) is a common technique for adapting pre-trained foundation models to specific tasks using labeled input-output pairs. Agentick unifies these paradigms by exposing all tasks through one Gymnasium-compatible interface so that RL agents, LLM/VLM agents, and humans can be compared directly.

## [AcuityBench: A New Benchmark for Clinical Acuity Identification in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11398) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced AcuityBench, a benchmark that harmonizes five public datasets into 914 cases under a shared four-level acuity framework ranging from home monitoring to immediate emergency care. It includes 697 consensus cases for standard accuracy and 217 physician-confirmed ambiguous cases for uncertainty-aware evaluation, and supports both QA classification and free-form conversational response formats. Acuity identification is a distinct safety-critical capability that existing health benchmarks do not evaluate in a unified way, so AcuityBench enables systematic comparison and stress-testing of how well models guide users to the right level of care. Its findings on over-triage versus under-triage tradeoffs and poor uncertainty alignment could directly inform the safe deployment of language models in real-world health settings. Across 12 frontier proprietary and open-weight models, the benchmark found substantial variation in clear-case acuity accuracy and error direction, with conversational responses reducing over-triage but increasing under-triage relative to QA, especially in higher-acuity cases. In ambiguous cases, no model closely matched the distribution of physician judgments, and model predictions were more concentrated than expert clinical uncertainty.

## [SORT: Selective Off-Policy Reference Tuning with Plan Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11505) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SORT introduces a repair update for GRPO-style reinforcement learning that targets hard prompts where all sampled rollouts fail. It derives a plan from the reference solution, compares token probabilities with and without that plan, and gives higher weight to tokens that become more predictable under plan conditioning, turning all-wrong prompts into selective, structure-aware learning signals instead of uniform imitation. This addresses a known limitation of GRPO-style reinforcement learning for reasoning tasks—stalling on hard prompts where all rollouts fail—and shows consistent improvements across three backbones and eight reasoning benchmarks, with the largest gains on weaker models. It could help improve reasoning performance in weaker or smaller language models, which is valuable for the broader AI/ML community. SORT adds a repair update without changing rollout generation, so it is compatible with existing GRPO-style pipelines. The method relies on reference solutions to derive plans and uses token-probability comparisons to weight learning signals, which means its effectiveness depends on the availability and quality of reference solutions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning method proposed by DeepSeek, specifically designed for large language model reinforcement learning, and is used to train reasoning models such as DeepSeek-R1. In GRPO-style training, the model samples multiple rollouts for each prompt and learns from their relative rewards. A known failure mode is that when all sampled rollouts for a hard prompt are wrong, the method provides no useful learning signal and training stalls.

## [BET Framework Cuts Reasoning Tokens 54% via Budget-Efficient Thinking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11625) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Budget-Efficient Thinking (BET), a two-stage framework combining behavioral cold-start with GRPO under an investment-cost-aware reward, which learns to allocate reasoning budget based on expected return rather than perceived difficulty. Across seven benchmarks and three base models, BET reduces reasoning tokens by 54% while improving accuracy by up to 3.2%, and transfers zero-shot to scientific QA and logical reasoning. This work addresses a key inefficiency in large reasoning models, which often overspend compute on unsolvable queries while under-spending on hard-but-solvable ones. By modeling adaptive reasoning as a computational investment under uncertainty, BET could influence future research on test-time compute allocation and make reasoning models more cost-effective in deployment. BET learns three distinct behaviors: short solve for easy queries, nice fold for early abstention when expected return is near zero, and hero call for preserving compute on hard-but-solvable queries. The framework uses rollout-derived solvability to align solve-or-fold decisions, and code is available at https://github.com/houqiii/BET.

**Background**: Large reasoning models (LRMs) improve problem solving by generating extended chains of thought, but they often misallocate test-time compute. Existing efficiency methods compress reasoning traces or condition budget on perceived difficulty, yet they treat pass rate as a difficulty score and ignore the zero-return regime. Test-time compute scaling, popularized by models like OpenAI's o1, allows models to think longer for better accuracy but at increased cost.

CODESKILL is an LLM-based framework that reformulates skill extraction and skill-bank maintenance for coding agents as a learnable management policy, trained with reinforcement learning using a hybrid reward of dense rubric-based skill-quality feedback and sparse verifiable execution feedback from a frozen downstream agent. On EnvBench, SWE-Bench Verified, and Terminal-Bench 2, it improves average pass rate by 11.03 over a no-skill baseline and by 5.10 over the strongest prompt-based or memory baseline while keeping a compact skill bank. Existing skill construction and maintenance methods rely on fixed prompts and heuristic update rules, leaving unclear how knowledge should be selected, abstracted, and maintained for downstream agents. By making this process learnable, CODESKILL points toward coding agents that genuinely self-evolve from their own trajectories, which could improve autonomous software-engineering performance across the ecosystem. CODESKILL extracts multi-granularity procedural skills from coding-agent trajectories, evolves them with new experience, and maintains a compact skill bank for future task solving. Its hybrid reward combines dense rubric-based skill-quality feedback with sparse verifiable execution feedback from the frozen downstream agent, and gains are measured on EnvBench, SWE-Bench Verified, and Terminal-Bench 2.

Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) is a paradigm that trains LLMs using rewards based on objectively verifiable outcomes rather than subjective human evaluations or learned reward models. ## [SciR: A Controllable Benchmark for Scientific Reasoning in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13020) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced SciR, a benchmark that generates scientific reasoning tasks from formal objects (deduction trees, inductive rule hypotheses, and causal graphs) and renders them into multi-document scientific discourse. It is the first multi-paradigm scientific-reasoning benchmark with parametric control over both extraction and inference difficulty, tested on six models. Existing scientific benchmarks rely on costly human annotations without mechanistic ground truth, while synthetic logical benchmarks do not resemble real scientific documents. SciR fills this gap by providing verifiable answers and controllable difficulty, which could improve how researchers evaluate and compare LLMs on scientific reasoning. The benchmark independently varies two difficulty axes—how hard it is to extract key information and how hard the principled inference itself is—and finds that both axes hurt every model, with compounding effects. The rendering even degrades neurosymbolic pipelines that hand inference to a verified solver, and reasoning models like deepseek-r1 mostly surpass non-reasoning instruct models on the inference axis.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Scientific reasoning involves three paradigmatic forms of inference: deduction (deriving conclusions from premises), induction (forming general rules from examples), and causal abduction (inferring the best explanation for observed effects). Evaluating LLMs on these forms is challenging because real scientific documents are complex and lack ground-truth reasoning traces. SciR addresses this by generating tasks from formal objects that guarantee verifiable answers, then rendering them into realistic scientific discourse.

## [Neural Networks Preserve Recoverable Case Structure, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11347) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.11347v2) establishes a theoretical connection between trained neural networks and Case-Based Decision Theory (CBDT), showing that networks can preserve a recoverable case structure through their learned representations. This structure allows fitted decision margins to be decomposed into individual case contributions, with the authors identifying conditions under which the recovered structure admits a CBDT interpretation and proving decision consistency with the original network's output. This work offers a new lens for neural network interpretability by tracing model decisions back to the specific training cases that support or oppose them, which could help assess the reliability of AI systems used in consequential decisions. It bridges interpretability research and decision theory, potentially giving users a principled way to verify whether a model has learned reliable decision patterns from data. The paper validates its approach on a controlled CBDT setting and three decision tasks based on real-world data, and it explicitly identifies the conditions under which the recovered case structure admits a CBDT interpretation. However, the abstract is dense and lacks immediate practical demonstrations, so the method's scalability to large models remains to be seen.

**Background**: Neural networks are often criticized as 'black boxes' because their internal mechanisms provide little evidence of how decisions relate to training data. Case-Based Decision Theory (CBDT) is a decision framework in which choices are made by comparing a current situation to past cases and weighing their outcomes. Interpretability research has explored case-based reasoning to explain deep neural networks by identifying similar exemplars, but a formal theoretical link between trained networks and CBDT had not been established.

<a id="item-195"></a>
## [ADMITOR: Label-Free Admission Gate for LLM Optimization Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15565) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ADMITOR introduces a label-free admission gate that generates models from three model families, runs each on the stated problem and on resampled-parameter instances, and keeps the largest group whose optimal values agree across families on every instance. Using a threshold fitted on solver-verified problems, it accepts, abstains, or escalates with a finite-sample bound on the false-discovery rate among accepted values, raising candidate-level admission precision to 0.927 versus 0.871 for majority vote and 0.726 for execution success. This matters because experience-learning agents that store and reuse solved trajectories can repeatedly retrieve a wrong trajectory, and without ground-truth answers on new problem streams there is no reliable way to reject it. ADMITOR offers a practical, label-free certification mechanism that improves the reliability of LLM-based optimization agents and could generalize to other skill-library settings where labels are unavailable. Inside a state-of-the-art skill learner, ADMITOR's library is the smallest of four yet reaches the highest macro accuracy over five public benchmarks (58.4 vs. 54.8 for majority vote). An ablation shows the gain comes from the accepted value being external to the learner and unanimous across families; resampling never changed an accepted value and only reduced coverage, and the false-discovery bound holds on the calibration set but not on the benchmark stream, where an audit traces most false certificates to benchmark texts that omit or round needed numbers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Experience learning for LLM-based optimization modeling stores solved problem trajectories as reusable skills, but a wrong trajectory admitted into the library can be retrieved repeatedly. Existing learners admit trajectories by matching known optima or labels, while label-free substitutes such as execution success or agreement at a single instance can admit wrong models. False-discovery-rate control is a standard statistical technique for bounding the expected proportion of false positives among accepted hypotheses, and ADMITOR adapts this idea to label-free trajectory admission.

<a id="item-196"></a>
## [Verifier-Selected Self-Training Boosts Workflow Repair Reliability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18324) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.18324v2) shows that verifier-admitted outputs from Qwen3-14B thinking mode can supervise the same checkpoint for non-thinking execution on structure-disjoint PlanBench replanning cases, raising VAL acceptance from 1/80 to 57/80. A prospective replication across eight LoRA seeds and three inference realizations per seed produced a clear lift for every seed (45/80 to 70/80), with an exact seed-level sign-flip test yielding p=0.0078125. This work suggests that machine-verifiable governance records can consolidate an expensive, occasional capability into reliable one-shot execution without oracle targets or a stronger teacher, which matters for teams building dependable AI workflow automation. It also draws a careful boundary: the evidence supports robust consolidation of one fixed, machine-checkable capability, not arbitrary planning or unrestricted self-improvement. Target-selection performance was far less stable: an initial matched seed gave 102/160 accepted plans after VAL selection versus 69/160 after blinded model self-selection, but across eight prospective seeds this contrast was seed-dependent, included one clear reverse seed, and did not replicate (p=0.3672). VAL also had a positive descriptive aggregate over schema-only selection but failed its preregistered seed-level reliability gate (p=0.0703), so the verifier remains the admission authority and no reliable downstream advantage of semantic selection is established.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: PlanBench is a benchmark that evaluates large language models on formal PDDL planning and reasoning tasks, including replanning scenarios. VAL is an independent plan validation tool originally developed for the International Planning Competition that automatically checks whether generated plans are correct. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method that freezes pretrained weights and injects small trainable low-rank matrices, allowing many cheap adaptation runs such as the eight seeds used here. The paper's "governance records" link a task contract, model attempt, verifier decision, accepted output, and target origin, forming the supervision signal for self-training.

## [PhysElite: A New Olympiad-Level Physics Benchmark for Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25097) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers released PhysElite, a large-scale bilingual multimodal benchmark containing 11,586 Olympiad-tier physics problems, each with visual diagrams, step-by-step Chinese-English solution derivations, and final answers. Benchmarking 18 open-source and closed-source multimodal LLMs revealed that even the strongest model achieves only 33.7% answer accuracy, with additional step-level process evaluation diagnosing where models fail in the reasoning chain. Existing physics benchmarks suffer from insufficient difficulty and incomplete coverage of visual forms, knowledge points, and solution processes, so model scores on them may overstate real expert-level reasoning ability. PhysElite provides a harder, more representative yardstick that could reshape how the community measures progress toward expert-level physical reasoning in multimodal LLMs. The benchmark is bilingual (Chinese-English) and multimodal, pairing each problem with visual diagrams and step-by-step derivations rather than only final answers. Beyond answer accuracy, the authors perform step-level process evaluation to pinpoint reasoning failures, and the dataset is publicly released on Hugging Face.

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) integrate language reasoning with modality-specific encoders to process text, images, audio, and video. Olympiad-level physics problems are considered a demanding test of expert reasoning because they require combining physical laws, mathematical derivation, and interpretation of diagrams. Prior benchmarks such as OlympiadBench and ABench-Physics also target advanced scientific reasoning, but PhysElite aims to address their limitations in difficulty and coverage.

## [T-LoopFormer adds token-level elastic recursion depth to looped Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15160) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (arXiv:2609.15160v2) proposes T-LoopFormer, which replaces the fixed recursion depth of looped Transformers with dynamic token-choice routing, letting each token decide how many loop iterations it needs based on its hidden state. It also introduces a recursion-wise KV cache that keeps a separate key-value cache per recursion loop so tokens at different depths only attend to their matching cached states, with code released on GitHub. Fixed uniform recursion depth wastes compute on easy tokens while under-processing hard ones, so token-level elastic depth could improve both accuracy and inference efficiency for latent-reasoning models. If validated, this kind of adaptive compute allocation is relevant to anyone deploying looped or recurrent-depth Transformers for reasoning and long-context generation. The router is a token-choice mechanism that adaptively sets loop iterations per token from its hidden state, and the recursion-wise KV cache is what makes faster autoregressive decoding possible by preventing cross-depth attention to mismatched cached states. Experiments report robust language modeling and zero-shot reasoning results plus the lowest decoding latency, though as an arXiv preprint the claims still await independent replication.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Looped Transformers reuse a single shared set of parameters across multiple iterations instead of stacking many distinct layers, gaining parameter efficiency and strong reasoning ability. Latent reasoning means the model performs its intermediate computation inside hidden states rather than emitting explicit chain-of-thought tokens, which saves tokens at inference time. Prior looped models applied the same recursion depth to every token, which is the inefficiency T-LoopFormer targets.

<a id="item-199"></a>
## [RLDS Decomposes Trajectory Rewards into Per-Subtask Advantages for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27035) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.27035v2) introduces Reinforcement Learning with Decomposed Subtasks (RLDS), whose core component Subtask-Decomposed Advantage Estimation (SDAE) replaces the single scalar GRPO advantage with per-subtask group-relative advantages on a fixed taxonomy, distributing per-token credit weighted by subtask importance and concentrated around reflection steps. On four agentic benchmarks, RLDS gains +11.5 points on ScienceWorld and +9.8 points on FrozenLake over scalar GRPO, while results on HotpotQA and DeepResearch stay within noise. The work targets a known weakness of GRPO-style policy-gradient training for multi-turn language model agents: collapsing an entire rollout into one scalar reward is lossy when a task composes distinct skills under sparse, delayed feedback. If decomposition reliably helps, it could change how reward signals are designed for agentic RL, especially for long-horizon tasks where credit assignment is hardest. Gains scale with subtask heterogeneity: the largest improvements appear on the high-heterogeneity tasks ScienceWorld (+11.5, paired-bootstrap 95% CI [+9.8, +13.3]) and FrozenLake (+9.8, [+7.0, +12.8]), while HotpotQA and DeepResearch show no significant change, matching the paper's own heterogeneity diagnostics. RLDS is also more compute-efficient on ScienceWorld, using 10.9% less wall-clock time per step than scalar GRPO because long rollouts amortize the fixed reflect-and-grade overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization), popularized by DeepSeek, trains language models by sampling a group of responses per prompt and using the group's mean reward as a baseline for advantages, avoiding the separate critic network that PPO requires. In multi-turn agent settings, the model interacts with tools and environments over many steps, and the final outcome reward must be attributed back to individual actions — a credit-assignment problem that becomes harder as tasks grow longer and feedback sparser. RLDS proposes splitting that reward along a fixed subtask taxonomy before the policy update, rather than trying to learn a better single scalar.

## [PUBG Ally: A Conversational Embodied AI Teammate in Live Gameplay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29837) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Krafton's PUBG team introduced PUBG Ally, a voice-enabled embodied AI teammate for PUBG: BATTLEGROUNDS that combines a language-model agent with a real-time control layer to perceive, reason, speak, and act alongside human players. The system was trained on data from nearly 39,000 live gameplay sessions and evaluated through player surveys across 141 countries, where positive responses exceeded negative ones by 25.1 percentage points on whether players would recommend Ally. This is a production-scale deployment of an embodied conversational agent inside a latency-constrained commercial game, showing that LLM-driven teammates can move from research demos to live service. It matters for game studios, embodied-AI researchers, and anyone studying human-AI collaboration, because it demonstrates a practical pipeline from gameplay data collection to on-device inference and safety guardrails. Ally uses a language-model agent with a controlled tool interface to inspect game state, interpret player speech, maintain context, and issue high-level action choices that steer a faster control layer for movement, combat, and recovery. To meet live-service requirements, the team applied model compression, context compaction, targeted safety training, runtime guardrails, and memory redaction for low-latency on-device execution and safe player-facing communication.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: An embodied agent is an AI system that interacts with an environment through a body or virtual representation, and an embodied conversational agent can also talk with humans using natural language. In games, such agents must perceive a constantly changing world and act under strict latency limits, which is hard because language models are typically slower and less deterministic than traditional game AI. PUBG: BATTLEGROUNDS is a large-scale battle royale shooter where teammates must coordinate movement, combat, and communication in real time, making it a demanding testbed for AI teammates.

## [PrivDrift Benchmark Reveals LLMs Leak User Secrets After Topic Drift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30094) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced PrivDrift, a benchmark of 1,000 controlled multi-turn dialogues with seeded secrets, content-dense drift turns, and standardized extraction probes, to audit whether user-disclosed secrets remain recoverable after conversational topic drift. Across three LLMs with extended context windows, dialogue-level hybrid leakage ranged from 38.7% to 54.6%, and additional topic drift did not reliably reduce leakage within the tested window. This work reframes LLM privacy risk as a persistent behavioral failure mode rather than only training-data memorization or immediate jailbreak behavior, which matters for anyone deploying persistent assistants in shared-session or tool-augmented settings. It suggests that current privacy evaluations may underestimate risk in active, long-running conversations. Leakage varied strongly by model, secret type, and persuasion intensity, and dialogue-level leakage can exceed the average of individual persuasion-level leakage rates because each dialogue contributes one response per persuasion condition. The benchmark uses persuasion-based probing and fuzzy-matching analyses at the probe level.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are increasingly used as persistent assistants that retain context across long, multi-turn conversations, including shared-session and tool-augmented deployments. When users disclose sensitive information in such conversations, that information may remain behaviorally recoverable through later prompts even after the dialogue shifts to unrelated topics. PrivDrift is designed to measure this recoverability systematically using seeded secrets and standardized extraction probes.

## [AD-WM: Action-Discriminative World Models Boost Counterfactual MPC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30264) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AD-WM introduces an action-discriminative joint-embedding world model that combines residual latent dynamics with predictor-level action-recovery regularization, using inverse dynamics and a normalized recovery objective motivated by conditional mutual information. On OGBench-Cube, it improves hard-start success from 3.7% to 52.0% over a matched LeWM baseline, and with a frozen V-JEPA 2 encoder it raises zero-shot Franka pick-and-place success from 42.2% to 71.1%. This work challenges the common assumption that lower factual prediction error automatically yields better planning, showing that world models for MPC should preserve action-dependent differences needed for counterfactual selection. If validated, it could meaningfully improve model-based reinforcement learning for robotic manipulation and other control tasks. The auxiliary heads used for inverse dynamics and action-recovery regularization are discarded at test time, leaving the MPC procedure unchanged, and planning diagnostics show that factual prediction error and whole-bank action ranking do not track closed-loop success, whereas CEM-aligned elite regret does. The paper is an arXiv preprint without peer review or community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: World models are learned neural simulators that predict future states given actions, and they are often used inside model predictive control (MPC), where a planner compares candidate action sequences from the same state. OGBench is a benchmark for offline goal-conditioned reinforcement learning with manipulation tasks such as Cube, while V-JEPA 2 is a video-pretrained encoder used here to support zero-shot transfer to a real Franka robot.

A new arXiv paper (2502.19784v3) presents NaijaNLP, the first comprehensive survey of NLP research across Nigeria's three major languages — Hausa, Yoruba, and Igbo — reviewing 293 studies. It quantitatively assesses available linguistic resources, finding that only 27.6% of the reviewed studies contributed new resources, revealing a heavy reliance on repurposing existing data. This survey provides the first coherent overview of a fragmented research landscape, helping researchers and funders identify where resources are missing and where collaborative effort is most needed. It could guide future work not only for NaijaNLP but for low-resource NLP more broadly, especially for African language technology. The survey highlights that language-specific challenges such as morphological analysis and effective representation of diacritics remain under-explored, and it calls for more collaborative efforts in resource enrichment, comprehensive annotation, and increased community support. The three languages are spoken by more than 175 million people but are still classified as low-resource due to insufficient digital resources.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

Low-resource languages lack the large annotated corpora and digital tools needed to train modern NLP models, so researchers often rely on cross-lingual transfer learning or repurposed data. Computational linguistics spans everything from grammatical formalisation to building the linguistic resources (lexicons, corpora, annotation schemes) that support model development.

Researchers released MedHal, a large-scale synthetic dataset specifically built to train and evaluate models for detecting hallucinations in medical texts, covering both intrinsic and extrinsic hallucination types across diverse medical sources and tasks. A baseline detection model trained on MedHal outperforms general-purpose hallucination detection approaches. ## [Tokenadapt and Supertoken Learning Enable Tokenizer Flexibility in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09738) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2505.09738v2) introduces Tokenadapt, a model-agnostic tokenizer transplantation method, and Supertoken learning for multi-word tokens. Tokenadapt initializes new token embeddings via a hybrid heuristic combining local subword decomposition and global top-k semantic similarity, achieving at least a 2-fold improvement in zero-shot perplexity ratio over ReTok and outperforming TransTokenizer baselines. This work addresses tokenizer lock-in, a major limitation of pretrained language models that causes inefficiency in multilingual and specialized domains. By enabling tokenizer flexibility with minimal retraining, it could significantly reduce computational costs and improve performance for non-English languages and domain-specific applications. Tokenadapt uses a hybrid heuristic: a local estimate based on subword decomposition with the old tokenizer and a global estimate using top-k semantically similar tokens from the original vocabulary. Supertokens aggregate multi-word units to enhance compression and reduce fragmentation, with empirical results showing notable compression gains and lower perplexity ratios across different base models and target tokenizers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Pretrained language models typically use a fixed tokenizer, meaning the model's vocabulary and segmentation rules are set during pretraining and cannot be easily changed. This 'tokenizer lock-in' makes it hard to adapt models to new languages or specialized domains without expensive retraining. Tokenizer transplantation aims to replace the tokenizer while preserving model performance, but existing methods often require extensive fine-tuning. Tokenadapt and Supertoken learning are new techniques designed to make this process more efficient and effective.

## [Neural Bridge Processes Anchor Diffusion to Inputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07220) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2508.07220v4) introduces Neural Bridge Processes (NBPs), which replace the input-independent forward kernel of Neural Diffusion Processes (NDPs) with an input-anchored bridge trajectory. When input and output dimensions differ, NBP learns an output-space anchor a_ψ(x)=P_ψ(x), letting inputs guide the generative path without altering the denoising backbone. This work strengthens conditional stochastic function modeling by injecting input information directly into noisy training states and creating a direct gradient pathway unavailable to NDPs. It could benefit researchers in probabilistic modeling, generative AI, and any application requiring uncertainty-aware predictions from partially observed data. The authors provide theoretical analysis showing that process-level anchoring induces pathwise input distinguishability and injects information about x into noisy states. Experiments on synthetic regression, EEG, CylinderFlow, and image regression show consistent improvements, and ablations confirm the gains come from the full bridge construction with learned alignment; the same principle also transfers to Flow Matching Neural Processes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Neural Diffusion Processes (NDPs) use denoising diffusion to model stochastic functions, but their forward process is input-independent, meaning inputs only enter the reverse denoiser and noisy training states do not encode conditioning inputs. Diffusion bridge models, in contrast, explicitly connect clean and degraded distributions via trajectories, and NBPs adapt this bridge idea to neural processes by anchoring the forward path to the input.

## [Provable Speech Attribute Conversion via Latent Independence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05191) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2510.05191v3) develops a formal framework for speech attribute conversion, establishing sufficient conditions for exact and consistent transfer under a deterministic autoencoder setting with an independence constraint between the latent representation and the controllable attribute. The authors also propose a practical voice conversion method implementing these principles, achieving competitive performance on voice and pitch conversion tasks. This work addresses a key gap in disentangled representation learning by providing theoretical guarantees for when reliable attribute control is achievable, moving beyond heuristic objectives common in speech style transfer. It could influence future research in controllable generation across audio, vision, and multimodal domains by offering a principled foundation for attribute manipulation. The analysis relies on explicit population-level assumptions about the data-generating process and links reconstruction, independence, and feasibility of attribute manipulation while preserving task-relevant content. The proposed method directly implements the core principles and is evaluated on voice and pitch conversion, showing applicability to real-world settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Disentangled representation learning aims to learn latent representations where each dimension corresponds to a single interpretable factor of variation, which is critical for controlled generation. In speech processing, this is often applied to style transfer, but most existing approaches rely on heuristic objectives and architectural choices without theoretical understanding of when reliable attribute control is achievable. This paper provides a formal framework to analyze such conditions, focusing on a deterministic autoencoder with an independence constraint between the latent representation and the controllable attribute.

Training only the top-10 predicted architectures brings performance within 2% of full-scale training of 2,000 randomly sampled architectures, yielding substantial computational savings. The study evaluates eight ZCPs on six HAR benchmark datasets and compares predicted rankings against full training of 2,000 randomly sampled architectures. The top-predicted architecture stays within 7% of full-training performance, and training the top-10 narrows the gap to 2%, though the approach is validated only on these specific HAR benchmarks and may not generalize to all sensor setups.

SLMFix is a new code generation pipeline that finetunes a small language model (SLM) with reinforcement learning to fix syntactic errors in LLM-generated programs for domain-specific languages (DSLs), using interpreter feedback as the reward signal. Experiments across multiple DSLs show a 40% improvement in validator pass rates on low-resource programming languages (LRPLs) and elimination of more than 50% of syntactic errors for high-resource DSLs. The approach relies on interpreter feedback rather than human labels, and notably outperforms supervised finetuning even for 7B models on LRPLs including Ansible and Lean. ## [Dynamic Pooled Testing Achieves at Most Twice Static Welfare](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22419) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper proves that for budget-constrained pooled testing with heterogeneous utilities and independent health priors, the optimal dynamic policy achieves at most twice the welfare of the optimal static overlapping allocation, for any population, test budget, and pool-size cap. The proof combines path sampling, random thinning of pools, a Boolean-cube argument on uniform subcubes, and a complementary-profile coupling to extend the result to arbitrary heterogeneous product priors. This result quantifies the maximum benefit of adaptivity in pooled testing, showing that dynamic re-pooling can never improve welfare by more than a factor of two over a static plan. It provides a theoretical foundation for deciding when adaptive testing strategies are worth the added complexity in resource-constrained settings such as public health screening. The paper also identifies regimes where adaptivity has no value, shows that strict adaptive gains require re-pooling agents after positive tests, and gives a three-agent instance where adaptation is strictly beneficial. It proves that Exact-Joint Greedy and the static non-overlapping Greedy algorithm of Finster et al. each achieve a 1/(e+1) fraction of optimal static overlapping welfare, which combined with the factor-two theorem implies they are 2(e+1)-approximations to the optimal dynamic policy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Pooled testing (also called group testing) combines samples from multiple individuals into a single test; if the pool tests negative, all individuals are certified healthy, saving tests when the disease prevalence is low. Adaptive procedures choose the next pool based on previous test outcomes, while non-adaptive procedures fix all pools in advance. This paper studies how much welfare—earned when an agent is certified healthy—can be improved by adaptivity under a budget constraint and heterogeneous agent utilities.

## [Auto-Comp Diagnoses Compositional Binding Failures in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02043) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Auto-Comp, a fully automated, concept-driven pipeline that generates photorealistic compositional benchmarks at scale using a parallel A/B construction: for each concept it emits a Minimal sample (template caption, isolated objects on a white background) and a Contextual sample (LLM-rewritten caption, objects embedded in a realistic scene). It instantiates four task families—Color and Shape-Color (attribute binding) and Position and Relative Size (relational binding)—and evaluates over 25 VLMs spanning CLIP, SigLIP, hard-negative-trained, and frontier generative models. Compositional reasoning is a known weak point of modern VLMs, and existing benchmarks either use noisy real images that conflate confounding visual variables with reasoning failure or simplistic synthetic scenes lacking realism. Auto-Comp's automated, scalable pipeline provides a cleaner diagnostic tool that could standardize how the field measures and improves binding ability across architectures and scales. The findings are consistent across architectures and scales: every model exhibits a large Swap-vs-Confusion gap, with low-entropy distractors (e.g., repeated objects or colors) exposing failures beyond the known bag-of-words limitations. The authors further uncover a task-dependent trade-off: visio-linguistic context aids relational reasoning but hinders attribute binding through visual clutter, and they publicly release the pipeline and benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) such as CLIP and SigLIP are trained to match images with text and are widely used for multimodal tasks. Compositional binding refers to correctly associating attributes (like color or shape) with the right object and relations (like position or relative size) with the right referents—something these models often fail at, sometimes called the "bag-of-words" limitation. Benchmarks are test suites used to measure such abilities, but designing them is hard because real images contain many confounding factors while synthetic images may be too unrealistic for models tuned on natural data.

## [Spectral-Sphere-Constrained Hyper-Connections Fix Doubly Stochastic Limits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20896) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2603.20896v2) identifies three limitations of the doubly stochastic constraint used in Manifold-Constrained Hyper-Connections (mHC) — identity degeneration, an expressivity bottleneck, and parameterization inefficiencies — and proposes Spectral-Sphere-Constrained Hyper-Connections (s²HC), which confines residual matrices to a spectral norm sphere. Hyper-Connections are an emerging architecture for enriching residual streams in deep networks, and mHC's doubly stochastic constraint was meant to keep training stable; by removing that constraint's expressivity bottleneck, s²HC could enable more expressive yet still stable deep models, affecting researchers working on neural architecture design and large-scale training stability. The paper argues that doubly stochastic residual matrices collapse around identity initialization, restrict the subdominant spectrum so the model cannot selectively preserve or attenuate cross-stream variations, and suffer from unstable Sinkhorn-Knopp iterations or factorial-scaling overhead in permutation-based parameterizations; s²HC instead constrains matrices to a spectral norm sphere, eliminating SK iterations and factorial parameterization while preserving training stability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Residual connections add a layer's input to its output, which helps gradients flow in very deep networks. Hyper-Connections extend this idea into multiple parallel streams mixed by residual matrices, but unconstrained mixing breaks the identity mapping and destabilizes training. Manifold-Constrained Hyper-Connections (mHC) addressed this by forcing those matrices to be doubly stochastic — rows and columns each sum to one — using the Sinkhorn-Knopp algorithm or permutation-based parameterizations. This paper analyzes the limitations of that doubly stochastic constraint and proposes a spectral-sphere alternative.

## [GVCC: Zero-Shot Video Compression via Codebook-Driven Stochastic Rectified Flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26571) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose GVCC, a zero-shot video compression framework that repurposes a pretrained video generative model as the decoder and transmits information through per-step stochastic innovations in a rectified flow process. The method converts a deterministic ODE-based flow sampler into an equivalent marginal-preserving stochastic process, and is instantiated in three modes: Text-to-Video, autoregressive Image-to-Video with tail latent correction, and First-Last-Frame-to-Video with boundary-sharing GOP chaining. This work addresses the challenge of ultra-low bitrate video compression, where high-fidelity reconstruction requires sampling plausible videos from the posterior rather than regressing to oversmoothed conditional means. By turning a pretrained generative model into a decoder, it could influence future research in generative compression and offer a new paradigm for zero-shot codecs. GVCC is evaluated on the seven-sequence UVG dataset, where local atom-count sweeps characterize rate-quality behavior of all three variants; the authors report full-dataset perceptual and fidelity metrics plus temporal diagnostics, but explicitly avoid inferring matched-rate or global rate-distortion improvements from these limited local sweeps. The approach relies on converting deterministic rectified-flow sampling into a stochastic process to create a per-step channel for transmitting compressed information.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Rectified flow, also known as flow matching, is a generative modeling approach based on ordinary differential equations (ODEs) that learns a deterministic mapping between noise and data. Diffusion models are a related class of latent variable generative models that generate data by iteratively denoising. Zero-shot video compression means using a pretrained model without task-specific training, and codebook-driven refers to transmitting information via discrete codes or innovations.

A new arXiv paper (2604.15186v2) introduces Scepsy, a serving system that schedules arbitrary multi-LLM agentic workflows onto a GPU cluster by profiling each LLM under different parallelism degrees and combining those profiles into an Aggregate LLM Pipeline. Scepsy reports up to 2.5x higher throughput before saturation and 1.0-3.3x lower latency than systems that optimize LLMs independently or rely on user-specified allocations. Agentic workflows that orchestrate many LLMs are increasingly common, but their data-dependent branching, fan-out, and recursion make execution times unpredictable and cause GPU oversubscription, so a scheduler that handles this complexity could substantially improve serving efficiency. This work matters for ML systems researchers and practitioners running multi-LLM agents in production, where throughput and latency directly affect cost and user experience. Scepsy's core insight is that while end-to-end agentic workflow latency is unpredictable, each LLM's fraction of execution time remains comparatively stable across requests, allowing a lightweight predictor to search over fractional GPU shares, tensor parallelism degrees, and replica counts. A hierarchical heuristic then places the chosen allocation onto the cluster to minimize fragmentation and respect network topology.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic workflows are applications in which multiple large language models and tools are orchestrated to complete complex tasks, often written in arbitrary agentic frameworks. Serving them is difficult because their execution branches, fans out, or recurs in data-dependent ways, and because the number of LLMs often exceeds available GPUs, causing oversubscription. Tensor parallelism is a common technique that distributes model weights and activations across multiple GPUs to reduce per-device memory requirements, and Scepsy profiles LLMs under different such parallelism degrees.

## [AI Agents Aggregate Information in Simple Prediction Markets but Hit Reasoning Ceiling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20050) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A controlled experiment (arXiv:2604.20050v4) had LLM-based AI agents trade in prediction markets after receiving private signals across four information structures of increasing complexity. Median markets aggregated information well in easy structures but deteriorated in harder ones, suggesting AI agents struggle when more than two levels of interactive reasoning are required — a ceiling close to that documented in human subjects. This finding suggests that even advanced AI agents may not reliably aggregate dispersed information in complex market environments, which matters for the design of AI-driven prediction markets, decentralized decision-making systems, and economic modeling. It also highlights a shared cognitive limitation between humans and AI, implying that scaling model capability alone may not overcome deep interactive reasoning barriers. Market accuracy did not improve from cheap talk communication, changing market duration, or strategic prompting, and giving agents feedback about past performance surprisingly did not improve aggregation. A follow-up wave three months later with capability-frontier models aggregated information more often in the three easier structures but not in the hardest one, where higher capability replaced confidently wrong markets with markets that hedge near 0.5.

## [VLAA-GUI Framework Boosts GUI Agent Reliability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.21375) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VLAA-GUI is a modular GUI agentic framework that integrates a mandatory Completeness Verifier, a mandatory Loop Breaker, and an on-demand Search Agent to address early stopping and repetitive loops. Evaluated across five top-tier backbones including Opus 4.5, 4.6, and Gemini 3.1 Pro on OSWorld and WindowsAgentArena, it achieves 77.5% and 61.0% respectively, with three backbones surpassing human performance (72.4%) on OSWorld in a single pass. This work tackles two fundamental failure modes of autonomous GUI agents—premature success claims and repetitive action loops—that currently limit their practical deployment. By improving reliability and nearly halving wasted steps for loop-prone models, it could accelerate the adoption of GUI agents in real-world automation tasks across Linux and Windows environments. The Completeness Verifier enforces UI-observable success criteria at every finish step, rejecting completion claims lacking direct visual evidence, while the Loop Breaker uses multi-tier filtering to switch interaction modes and force strategy changes after repeated failures. Ablation studies show all three components consistently improve a strong backbone, with weaker backbones benefiting more when given a sufficient step budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: GUI agents are AI systems that perceive and interact with graphical user interfaces to automate tasks, such as clicking buttons or filling forms. Two common failure modes are early stopping, where an agent declares success without evidence, and repetitive loops, where it repeats failing actions. VLAA-GUI is a modular framework that adds verification, loop-breaking, and search capabilities to standard agent architectures to mitigate these issues.

## [Human-1: First Open Full-Duplex Hindi Spoken Dialogue System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23295) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Josh Talks introduces Human-1, the first open, reproducible full-duplex spoken dialogue system for Hindi, built by adapting the Moshi duplex speech architecture with a custom Hindi tokenizer and trained on 26,000 hours of real spontaneous conversations from 14,695 speakers. The system uses a two-stage training recipe of large-scale pre-training followed by fine-tuning on 1,000 hours of conversational data, and evaluation via prompted dialogue continuation shows it generates natural full-duplex behavior in Hindi. This is a significant step for conversational AI in low-resource languages, as full-duplex systems have remained largely unexplored for Indian languages despite their importance for natural interaction. It could pave the way for real-time duplex spoken dialogue systems in Hindi and other Indian languages, benefiting hundreds of millions of speakers. The team replaced Moshi's original English tokenizer with a custom Hindi tokenizer and reinitialized text-vocabulary-dependent parameters while retaining the pre-trained audio components. The dataset was collected with separate speaker channels, enabling direct learning of turn-taking and overlap patterns from natural interactions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Full-duplex spoken dialogue systems process listening and speaking concurrently, enabling dynamic turn negotiation, interruptions, overlapping speech, and backchannels—unlike traditional turn-based systems that alternate between listening and speaking. Moshi is a state-of-the-art open full-duplex speech architecture that uses parallel user/model audio streams plus an 'inner monologue' text stream to achieve low-latency speech-to-speech interaction. Backchannels are brief verbal or non-verbal signals (like 'mm-hmm' or nods) that listeners use to indicate attention without taking the floor.

## [StraTA Adds Trajectory-Level Strategies to Agentic RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06642) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Strategic Trajectory Abstraction (StraTA), a framework that samples a compact trajectory-level strategy from the initial task state and conditions subsequent actions on it, training strategy generation and action execution jointly with a hierarchical GRPO-style rollout design. StraTA reaches 93.1% success on ALFWorld, 84.2% on WebShop, and a 63.5% overall score on SciWorld, outperforming frontier closed-source models. Current LLM agents are largely purely reactive, which weakens exploration and credit assignment over long horizons; by making strategy explicit, StraTA improves both sample efficiency and final performance, suggesting a practical path for building more capable long-horizon agents. The consistent gains across embodied, web, and scientific benchmarks indicate the approach could generalize to a wide range of agentic tasks. StraTA is trained with a hierarchical GRPO-style rollout design and further enhanced by diverse strategy rollout and critical self-judgment, which help the agent explore varied strategies and evaluate their quality. The framework is evaluated on ALFWorld, WebShop, and SciWorld, though the abstract does not detail computational overhead or the exact baselines used.

## [Transformers Can Implement Preconditioned Richardson Iteration for Gaussian Kernel Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08475) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.08475v3) proves both theoretically and empirically that a standard softmax-attention transformer can approximate kernel ridge regression (KRR) with Gaussian kernels during its forward pass. The authors construct a single-head transformer that approximately implements preconditioned Richardson iteration on the associated kernel system, using O(log(1/ε)) blocks and MLP width O(√(N/ε)) to achieve ε-accurate prediction for prompts of length N. This work offers a concrete mechanistic explanation for how transformers perform in-context learning, showing that depth corresponds to iterative refinement toward an exact Gaussian KRR solution. It bridges numerical linear algebra and deep learning theory, potentially guiding the design of more interpretable and efficient transformer architectures. The construction reveals a functional decomposition: softmax attention produces a row-normalized Gaussian-kernel operator for cross-token interactions, while MLP layers locally approximate the intra-token scalar arithmetic required by the update. Empirically, GPT-2-style transformers trained on Gaussian-process regression tasks progressively align with the exact Gaussian KRR estimator across depth in both prediction error and induced weights, and deeper layers match later iterates of classical KRR solvers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Kernel ridge regression (KRR) with a Gaussian kernel is a classic non-parametric regression method that yields a point estimate equal to the Gaussian process posterior mean. In-context learning refers to a transformer's ability to perform inference on the fly from examples in its prompt without weight updates. Preconditioned Richardson iteration is an iterative numerical method for solving linear systems, where a preconditioner transforms the problem into a more easily solvable form. This paper connects these concepts by showing that a transformer's forward pass can simulate such an iterative solver.

## [CaC: Hierarchical Spatiotemporal Reward Model for Video Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11723) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces CaC (Concentrate and Concentrate), a coarse-to-fine anomaly reward model built on Vision-Language Models that performs a global temporal scan, fine-grained spatial grounding, and structured spatiotemporal Chain-of-Thought reasoning. It also releases the first large-scale generated video anomaly dataset with per-frame bounding boxes, temporal windows, and attribution labels, and trains via a three-stage paradigm ending in two-turn GRPO with Temporal and Spatial IoU rewards. CaC reports a 25.7% accuracy gain on fine-grained anomaly benchmarks and, when used as a reward signal, reduces generated-video anomalies by 11.7% while improving overall video quality, suggesting reward models can directly improve video generation. This matters for researchers working on video understanding, reinforcement learning from rewards, and generative video quality control. The training pipeline first learns spatial and temporal anchoring through single- and multi-frame supervised fine-tuning, then applies two-turn GRPO; beyond conventional accuracy rewards, Temporal and Spatial IoU rewards supervise the intermediate localization process to encourage grounded and interpretable reasoning. The dataset is generated rather than captured from real surveillance footage, which may raise domain-gap concerns for real-world deployment.

GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement-learning algorithm popularized by DeepSeek that samples a group of responses per prompt and uses the group's mean reward as a baseline, avoiding the need for a separate critic model. Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning refers to prompting or training a model to produce intermediate reasoning steps, which CaC extends into the spatiotemporal domain for video.

Researchers introduced Grid-Orch, an LLM-powered orchestrator that connects large language models to power system simulation tools via the Model Context Protocol (MCP), using OpenDSS as its reference implementation. It provides 36 domain-specific tools across eleven categories and supports both cloud-hosted (Gemini, Claude) and locally deployed (Ollama, llama-cpp) models, enabling air-gapped operation for security-sensitive utilities. The power distribution engineering workforce faces a projected shortage of up to 1.5 million engineers by 2030, so making complex analyses accessible through natural language could significantly lower the barrier to entry. Grid-Orch's provider-agnostic and air-gapped design makes LLM-driven simulation practical for real utility environments, potentially accelerating workflows like distributed energy resource interconnection screening. Grid-Orch covers power flow, voltage analysis, quasi-static time series (QSTS) simulation, and automated optimization, and adds three optimization skills: capacitor placement, voltage violation analysis, and overvoltage mitigation. Workflow demonstrations show that analyses formerly requiring hours of scripting, such as DER interconnection screening, complete in under two minutes while producing numerically identical results to direct OpenDSS scripting.

## [DyMoS Rebalances Attention to Boost Motion in Image-to-Video Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19398) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.19398v4) identifies 'reference-frame dominance' as the key mechanism that makes image-to-video (I2V) models generate overly static videos, and proposes DyMoS (Dynamic Motion Slider), a training-free and model-agnostic method that rebalances the attention pathway from generated frames to the reference frame during the initial denoising steps. DyMoS leaves the input image and model weights unchanged and introduces only a single scalar parameter for continuous control over motion strength. This offers a practical, plug-and-play solution to a well-known limitation of I2V models—overly static outputs—without retraining or sacrificing fidelity to the reference image, making it attractive to researchers and practitioners working with existing state-of-the-art I2V backbones. It could also inform future attention-level designs for controllable video generation. The method works by rebalancing the attention pathway from generated frames to the reference frame during the initial denoising steps, and it is training-free and model-agnostic, requiring no changes to input images or model weights. Experiments across multiple state-of-the-art I2V backbones show consistent improvements in motion dynamics while maintaining visual quality and reference fidelity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Image-to-video (I2V) models generate a video from a single reference image, but they often produce videos that are much more static than those from text-to-video models. Prior fixes typically weaken or modify the image-conditioning signal, but these often require additional training or reduce fidelity to the reference image. This paper instead traces the problem to attention: non-reference frames allocate excessive self-attention to reference-frame key tokens, over-propagating reference information across time and suppressing inter-frame dynamics.

## [Cut-DeepONet Handles Discontinuities in Neural Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19823) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.19823v2) introduces Cut-DeepONet, a two-stage training framework that explicitly models discontinuities in neural operators by partitioning the domain into smooth subregions and representing discontinuities as boundaries in a higher-dimensional space. An additional network predicts input-dependent discontinuity locations for unseen inputs, guiding the operator to generate smooth components within each region. Neural operators are increasingly used as fast surrogates for PDE solvers, but their continuous representations struggle with discontinuities and sharp transitions, often requiring larger models and high-resolution data. Cut-DeepONet outperforms state-of-the-art methods on benchmark PDEs even with low-resolution training data and fewer trainable parameters, suggesting that changing the representation of operator learning may be more effective than simply scaling up model complexity. The method uses a lifting strategy that reformulates the problem so discontinuities become boundaries in a higher-dimensional space, aligning the learning task with the inductive bias of neural networks. Experiments show it excels on problems with discontinuities and sharp transitions while using fewer trainable parameters, though the abstract does not specify the exact benchmark PDEs or quantitative error metrics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Neural operators are a class of deep learning architectures that learn mappings between infinite-dimensional function spaces, primarily used to approximate the solution operators of partial differential equations (PDEs) much faster than traditional numerical solvers. DeepONet is a foundational 2021 operator learning architecture that uses a branch net for input functions and a trunk net for output locations to approximate nonlinear operators. However, because neural operators rely on continuous representations, they struggle to capture discontinuities and sharp transitions in PDE solutions, which excite a broad spectrum of frequencies and are inherently difficult for smooth function approximators.

## [Hide-and-Seek Detects VLA Execution Failures via Contrastive Trajectory Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30834) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Hide-and-Seek, a framework that formulates Vision-Language-Action (VLA) failure detection as a coarsely supervised learning problem, using inter-trajectory and intra-trajectory contrastive objectives to localize failure-indicative actions from trajectory-level labels alone. Evaluated on LIBERO, VLABench, and a real-world robot platform with OpenVLA, π0, and π0.5 policies, it achieves state-of-the-art multi-task failure detection with a practical accuracy-timeliness trade-off under conformal prediction. Reliable failure detection is critical for deploying embodied AI systems in the real world, where undetected execution errors can cause damage or unsafe behavior. By eliminating the need for expensive action resampling, external models, or step-level annotations, Hide-and-Seek offers a more practical and scalable path to runtime monitoring for VLA policies. The method induces temporally structured failure signals without any step-level annotation, addressing the limitation of prior approaches that propagate trajectory-level labels uniformly across every timestep and thereby obscure localized failure signals. It generalizes to both seen and unseen tasks, and the conformal prediction component provides a tunable accuracy-timeliness trade-off.

They are typically built by fine-tuning a vision-language model on large-scale datasets of paired visual observations, language instructions, and robot trajectories. ## [INFUSER: Influence-Guided Self-Evolution Boosts LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09052) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced INFUSER, an iterative co-training framework in which a Generator drafts questions and reference answers from unstructured documents while a Solver trains on them, with the Generator rewarded by an optimizer-aware influence score. To handle this continuous, noisy signal, they propose DuGRPO, a dual-normalized variant of GRPO, and report over 20% relative improvement on Olympiad and SuperGPQA benchmarks with Qwen3-8B-Base. This addresses a key weakness in self-evolution methods, where generators are often rewarded by difficulty heuristics that do not actually improve the solver, potentially making self-improvement more reliable and scalable with minimal external supervision. The result that an 8B co-evolving generator outperforms a frozen 32B thinking generator suggests smaller models can substitute for much larger ones in data generation. The influence score measures whether each proposed question would actually improve the solver on the target distribution, turning the document pool into an adaptive curriculum that favors useful questions rather than merely hard ones. Ablations confirm each design choice is necessary, and two extensions—applying INFUSER to an instruction-finetuned anchor and augmenting it with rule-verifiable RLVR data—demonstrate flexibility; code is available on GitHub.

Influence functions, originating in robust statistics and popularized in machine learning by a 2017 paper, estimate how a training data point affects model parameters and predictions. GRPO is a reinforcement learning algorithm used to train language models, and INFUSER adapts it for the noisy influence signal.

## [ADAS: Training-Free Reranking Boosts Masked Diffusion Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10829) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose ADAS, a training-free reranking rule for masked diffusion language models that discounts each token's confidence score based on its attention to already-selected positions, weighted by their prediction uncertainty. Plugged into Top-k, Fast-dLLM, and EB-Sampler, ADAS improves low-NFE performance by 9.11 and 10.46 percentage points on average on LLaDA-8B-Base and Dream-7B-Base across GSM8K, MATH500, HumanEval, and MBPP, with only 3.1% per-forward runtime overhead. This addresses a core fragility of parallel token revelation in masked diffusion language models, where individually confident tokens can be unsafe to commit together due to coupled predictions. The method is training-free, sampler-agnostic, and low-overhead, making it broadly applicable to efficient inference research and potentially to production deployment of diffusion-based text generation. ADAS leaves the base sampler's stopping rule unchanged and only reranks candidates, so it can be combined with existing samplers without retraining. The reported gains are at matched denoiser evaluations, and the code is available at https://github.com/yusufsahin99/ADAS.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Masked diffusion language models generate text by iteratively unmasking tokens rather than producing them strictly left-to-right, which allows multiple tokens to be revealed per denoising step. The number of function evaluations (NFE) measures how many denoising steps are used, and lower NFE means faster inference but often worse quality. Existing training-free samplers like Top-k, Fast-dLLM, and EB-Sampler mainly control how many tokens to reveal, while ranking candidates by token-wise scores that ignore interactions among the selected set.

## [Genetic Algorithms with Optimization-Guided Operators Analyzed via Query Complexity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12279) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces a general model of genetic algorithms in which mutation and recombination operators are guided by optimization rather than randomness, as used in ML inference-time search. The authors formulate optimization in this model as a query complexity problem using reinforcement learning language and prove three fundamental phenomena, including a diversity result for parity learning showing optimal query complexity of Θ(w+2^{n-w}) for pool size w and vectors of length n. This work bridges classical genetic algorithm theory with modern ML inference-time optimization, offering a rigorous framework to understand when and why guided operators help. It could influence how researchers design and analyze inference-time search methods in AI/ML and optimization, particularly regarding diversity and memory constraints. The paper shows that diversity of the solution pool can be necessary, that generation, mutation, and recombination can all be simultaneously necessary for near-optimal solutions, and that a positive drift of operators yields exponential speedup for Gaussian distributions. It also proves that Θ(n^2) bits of memory are necessary for efficient success under general memory constraints.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Genetic algorithms are a class of optimization methods inspired by natural evolution, traditionally using random mutation and recombination (crossover) operators to explore a population of candidate solutions. Recent ML approaches apply genetic algorithms at inference time, but replace random operators with optimization-guided ones that are more likely to improve the objective at higher computational cost. Query complexity measures how many evaluations of an objective function are needed to solve a problem, and reinforcement learning provides a language for sequential decision-making under uncertainty.

## [Embedding Model Routing Formalized as Adversarial Contextual Bandit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14929) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper arXiv:2606.14929v2 formalizes embedding model routing as an adversarial contextual linear bandit with low-rank experts, where queries are contexts, items are actions, and embedding models are experts operating on low-rank latent spaces. It introduces a log-quadratic policy class and a Hypentropy Policy Gradient (HPG) algorithm that achieves a linearized policy regret of O~(s√(MT)), where s is the intrinsic rank, M the number of models, and T the number of rounds. This work provides a rigorous theoretical foundation for routing queries to multiple embedding models in recommendation systems, addressing realistic challenges like adversarial queries and bandit feedback. It could lead to more efficient and robust online learning algorithms for model selection, benefiting large-scale recommendation and retrieval systems. The paper first shows that standard regret notions suffer from structural misspecification or statistical intractability in this setting, then identifies a log-quadratic policy class that is expressive yet structured for efficient learning. HPG is computationally efficient and parameter-free, provably adapting to unknown low-rank structure under incomplete information and avoiding the curse of dimensionality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Contextual bandits are a framework for sequential decision-making where an agent observes a context, chooses an action, and receives a reward, aiming to minimize regret. Adversarial contextual linear bandits extend this to settings where rewards or contexts may be chosen adversarially, and low-rank experts refer to models whose parameters lie in a low-dimensional subspace, enabling efficient learning. Embedding model routing involves dynamically selecting which embedding model to use for a given query, a common problem in modern recommendation systems.

## [IRT and Adaptive Testing Cut Safety Benchmark Costs by Up to 99.9%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20626) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.20626v2) proposes applying Item Response Theory (IRT) and adaptive item selection to language model safety benchmarks, showing that full-benchmark rankings can be approximated with Spearman's ρ > 0.90 while cutting evaluation cost by at least 80% on every benchmark where that threshold is attainable, and by up to 99.9% on AIR-Bench 2024. The authors also introduce a practical procedure for extracting a fixed, informative subset of items reusable across models, achieving savings of 80–99.8%. Safety benchmarks for modern language models are growing so large that full evaluation would require on the order of 10^5 responses, most of which provide little ranking signal, so this work could substantially reduce the cost and time of safety evaluations while preserving model rankings. It matters for AI labs, auditors, and regulators who need frequent, scalable safety assessments as benchmark suites expand. The paper analyzes six widely used safety benchmarks and shows that IRT recovers interpretable structure, with ability estimates resolving differences among models that cluster at the ceiling of raw safety metrics; adaptive selection dynamically chooses informative items per model, while the fixed-subset alternative is static and reusable across models. The 99.9% reduction is achieved specifically on AIR-Bench 2024, and the 80% floor applies only to benchmarks where the ρ > 0.90 threshold is attainable.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Item Response Theory (IRT) is a psychometric paradigm that models the probability of a correct or keyed response as a mathematical function of both person ability and item parameters such as difficulty and discrimination, rather than assuming all items are equally informative. Adaptive item selection, as used in computerized adaptive testing, dynamically chooses the most informative next item for each test taker based on prior responses. Spearman's rank correlation coefficient (ρ) measures how strongly two sets of rankings agree, making it a natural metric for checking whether a reduced benchmark preserves the full benchmark's model ordering.

Researchers introduced the Nanotechnology Molecular Optimization (NMO) Benchmark, which replaces proxy oracles with quantum simulations to drive generative molecular design toward real nanotechnology targets. The suite imposes hard structural constraints and rugged fitness landscapes, and notably, advanced molecular optimization methods underperform simpler approaches on these tasks. This benchmark bridges machine learning and quantum materials science, addressing the limited transferability of drug-discovery-focused models to structurally distinct domains. The NMO tasks use physics-based quantum simulations to calculate real molecular properties, eliminating pharmaceutical dataset bias. The authors developed a new baseline with a novel representation for structural constraints and a domain-agnostic pretraining strategy, surpassing state-of-the-art physical properties and revealing previously unknown structural motifs.

## [CoFL-S predicts language-conditioned sector flow fields for local navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02222) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CoFL-S introduces a low-level vision-language-action framework that predicts a language-conditioned flow field over the robot's local visible sector and rolls it out into continuous trajectories. The authors also release a continuous-time Habitat benchmark that isolates low-level action interfaces and executes all methods through a shared velocity-command controller. Vision-Language Navigation research has focused heavily on high-level instruction reasoning while leaving low-level action representation underexplored, so CoFL-S targets a real gap in embodied AI. Its decomposition-independent benchmark could become a standard way to compare low-level action interfaces across planner frequencies, benefiting robotics and embodied AI researchers. Training data is built by converting each VLN-CE episode, originally a whole-episode instruction with an action sequence, into frame-level local supervision with aligned sub-instructions and matched action, trajectory, and dense flow-field targets. Under matched encoders and training settings, CoFL-S consistently outperforms baselines across planner frequencies, and zero-shot real-world closed-loop deployment shows advantages beyond simulation.

Habitat is a widely used platform for training and benchmarking embodied AI agents in photorealistic 3D scenes. Flow fields are vector fields that describe motion direction and magnitude at each point, and here they are used as a dense, continuous action representation instead of discrete forward-and-turn commands.
