## [CheapCT Probes Predict Expensive 3D CT Encoder Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22771) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces CheapCT, a cheap probe on 3D CT encoder representations that reliably forecasts expensive downstream fine-tuning performance for report generation and MeasureVQA, a new VQA dataset built by the authors. The rank agreement between CheapCT and full fine-tuning stays high across every capability, ranging from rho = 0.90 to 0.97, and the forecast survives changes to the probe readout and the language-model backbone. Choosing an image encoder for a 3D CT vision-language model currently requires fine-tuning every candidate through the full language model, an enormously expensive search. CheapCT can pick an encoder nearly as good as the best while fine-tuning only a single candidate, at orders of magnitude less compute, which could significantly accelerate medical vision-language model development. CheapCT is a position-aware attention probe that reads the same visual tokens later consumed by a language model, and MeasureVQA scores outcomes one capability at a time using answers measured from segmentation masks and Hounsfield units, while report generation scores the whole report at once and reflects mostly disease. The code and MeasureVQA dataset are released at https://github.com/renjie-liang/CheapCT.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Building a 3D CT vision-language model begins with choosing which image encoder to build on, and today that choice is made by fine-tuning every candidate through the full language model and comparing downstream scores. A cheap probe on the encoder's representation promises a way out, but whether it actually forecasts the expensive outcome had never been tested before this work. Hounsfield units are a normalized scale of X-ray attenuation values in CT imaging, where bone has much higher values than soft tissue or air, making them useful for quantitative measurement.

## [Recover-and-Reguard Defense Closes VLM Encoded Jailbreak Decode Gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26574) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.26574v3) introduces a preprocessor that recovers and decodes encoded harmful requests before they reach a VLM safety classifier, raising block rates on image-rendered attacks from exactly 0% to 67-90%. Evaluated against an ensemble of eleven encoding attacks, the defense shows that restoring a view the guard never had is what buys coverage, though the benign cost depends on the guard rather than the mechanism. This work exposes a fundamental vulnerability in the dominant black-box defense for vision-language models: safety guards judge surface form, not meaning, so harmful requests re-encoded as set theory, formal logic, classical languages, code, or text-in-image slip past. By systematically quantifying the decode gap and showing that no configuration achieves both low attack success and low benign over-refusal, it sets a realistic bar for what recovery-based defenses can and cannot deliver. The evaluation uses an ensemble of eleven encoding attacks (six published implementations, one standard encoding baseline, one adapted, and three author-constructed renders), counting a behavior as broken if any attack succeeds. A reguard step that re-screens the recovered pre-decode surface lowers ensemble attack success but raises benign over-refusal on all ten guard-target pairs, and no configuration reaches an ensemble attack-success rate at or below 40% while holding benign over-refusal under 70%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) process both images and text, and their dominant black-box defense is a safety classifier or "guard" that screens inputs for harmful content. Because guards evaluate the surface form of an input rather than its underlying meaning, an attacker can re-encode a harmful request—for example as set theory, formal logic, a classical language, code, or text rendered inside an image—so that it passes the guard while remaining understandable to the model. This mismatch is called the decode gap, and the standard proposed fix is a preprocessor that recovers image content and decodes the encoding before the guard sees it.

## [Best-of-N Jailbreaking via Code Encoding Beats SAGE Defense](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26639) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.26639v2) shows that moving the query budget of best-of-N jailbreaking from surface character noise into a structural code-completion encoding achieves 67%, 22%, and 15% attack success on three open-weight targets against the SAGE self-check defense, versus at most 4.7% for the encoding alone and 3.0% for published character search at full budget — a 9 to 75x improvement. The result shows that SAGE, the strongest published self-check defense, fails under composition, and that fusing screening with generation into a single pass is architecturally unsafe; this has direct implications for how LLM safety defenses should be designed and evaluated. The paper reports operative figures at an actionable severity threshold (24, 8, and 1 behaviors with 95% CIs of [13,28], [3,13], and [0,3]), and a 2x2 ablation shows transform defenses are broken by encoding depth (7→67 behaviors at fixed variation) while gate defenses are broken by variation breadth (13→57 at fixed encoding).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Best-of-N jailbreaking spends a fixed query budget generating many surface-level variations of a harmful request until one bypasses the model's safety filter. SAGE is a published self-check defense that screens requests before answering, and code-completion encoding reframes a harmful request as a code task to route the model into code-completion mode. The paper isolates the encoding as the only difference between attack arms, holding the search procedure identical.

## [TSDS Framework Cuts Edge LLM Agent Reasoning Compute by Up to 65%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26865) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.26865v3) introduces Think Short, Defer Smart (TSDS), a framework for edge LLM agents that combines a lightweight convergence probe to halt on-device reasoning early with a perplexity-based deferral rule that escalates uncertain actions to a cloud model. Both mechanisms are jointly calibrated via a multi-objective Learn-Then-Test procedure, yielding simultaneous finite-sample guarantees on expected episode reward and cloud-call rate, and reducing per-episode thinking compute by 43%-65% over deferral-only baselines on HotpotQA, MBPP, and a household robot task. Deploying LLM agents on resource-constrained edge devices requires balancing reasoning quality against compute and connectivity costs, and TSDS offers a principled way to do so with statistical guarantees rather than ad-hoc thresholds. This could make ReAct-style agents more practical for latency-sensitive and privacy-conscious applications such as robotics and on-device assistants. TSDS is evaluated on four ReAct benchmarks—GSM8K (arithmetic reasoning), HotpotQA (multi-hop QA), MBPP (code generation), and a household robot embodied planning task—and is compared against thought-calibration-only and calibrated-deferral-only baselines. The joint calibration provides certified reward and cloud-call rate guarantees, though the abstract does not specify the exact guarantee levels or the overhead of the convergence probe itself.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: The ReAct paradigm lets LLM agents alternate between explicit chain-of-thought reasoning and external actions, which is powerful for multi-step tasks but computationally expensive. Edge deployment means running the agent on local hardware with limited compute, so a common strategy is to defer hard cases to a larger cloud model; however, deciding when to defer is difficult. Perplexity—a measure of how surprised a language model is by its own output—is often used as a proxy for uncertainty, and Learn-Then-Test is a statistical calibration method that can provide finite-sample guarantees on multiple objectives.

## [Counterfactual Recoverability Improves On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04408) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.04408v3) introduces counterfactual recoverability, a framework that replays each error state in on-policy distillation through budget-matched teacher-continuation and rollback branches to classify states as recoverable, irreversible-but-avoidable, or ambiguous. On AIME branch diagnostics, the mean continuation-minus-rollback effect is 0.185 for recoverable states versus -1.000 for irreversible-but-avoidable states, and a branch-derived recoverability proxy achieves an AUC of 1.000 compared with 0.392 for divergence alone. This work shows that not every divergence between student and teacher should be suppressed, offering a principled, outcome-grounded decision variable for selective supervision in on-policy distillation. It could meaningfully improve LLM training efficiency and final performance, as demonstrated by gains on held-out AIME2025 (0.578 vs. 0.517 for the best baseline) and improved average@32 on AIME2024-2025 and GPQA-Diamond. The method categorizes error states into recoverable, irreversible-but-avoidable, and ambiguous, then guides training to retain, roll back, or conventionally supervise the corresponding trajectory; ablations show that retaining teacher-correctable prefixes provides the largest individual contribution. Reported gains include AIME2024-2025 average@32 improving from 0.2656 to 0.3125 and GPQA-Diamond average@32 from 0.2702 to 0.3070.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation (OPD) is a knowledge distillation technique for large language models in which the student model generates its own trajectories through on-policy sampling, and a stronger teacher grades each token or step of those trajectories. Traditional OPD relies on divergence-based rules to decide when to correct the student, but such rules cannot tell whether an erroneous prefix is still correctable. This paper reframes that decision as a counterfactual question—what would happen if the student continued versus rolled back—and uses the answer to decide whether to retain, roll back, or conventionally supervise the trajectory.

## [Unified Dynamic FLD Unifies All N-Point Face Landmark Datasets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10346) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes Face Part-Anchored Landmark Positions (FPALPs), a representation that expresses each landmark as a progression value between 0 and 1 along a face part's contour, allowing all N-point face landmark datasets to be merged into a single dataset. Combined with a cross-modality decoder that iteratively refines FPALP-based landmark queries, the resulting Unified Dynamic FLD model trains on any number of N-point datasets and outputs any number of landmarks by loading designated queries at runtime. Face landmark detection has long been fragmented: each N-point benchmark requires its own trained parameters and can only predict its own N landmarks, which multiplies training cost and prevents knowledge sharing across datasets. A single unified model that remains competitive with or outperforms state-of-the-art methods could simplify FLD pipelines and make landmark detection more flexible for downstream face applications. Each landmark is represented by an FPALP-based query that a cross-modality decoder refines progressively, with coordinates predicted from the final representation; the number of predicted landmarks is controlled at runtime by loading the corresponding queries. The paper reports extensive experiments on multiple benchmark datasets showing these benefits while remaining competitive with, and in several cases outperforming, existing state-of-the-art methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Face landmark detection (FLD) predicts the coordinates of predefined key points on a face, and is a key step for tasks such as face recognition, emotion estimation, and face reconstruction. The number of landmarks and the exact mapping between each landmark and its facial location (the annotation scheme) are defined at the dataset level, so different benchmarks use incompatible N-point schemes. This incompatibility is why models have traditionally been tied to a single dataset's annotation scheme.

## [RIFT Eliminates Iterative Video Rollout in World Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11521) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RIFT (Rollout-free Imagination via Future Tokens) uses learned anticipation tokens to build a complete future K/V cache in a single backbone pass, removing the need for iterative video rollout at deployment. It achieves 98.8% overall success on LIBERO with a 3.1–9.2× inference speedup, and reaches 81.1% on the out-of-distribution LIBERO-Plus benchmark without further training. Iterative video rollout is a major source of deployment latency in world action models, so removing it while improving success rates could make future-conditioned robot policies far more practical for real-time control. The gains on out-of-distribution and real-world tasks suggest the approach generalizes beyond the simulation benchmarks where such methods are usually validated. Paired closed-loop interventions across four WAMs and 40 simulated manipulation tasks show that blocking or reassigning the future cache changes execution and reduces success, yet reusing one fixed final-clean K/V cache throughout action denoising nearly preserves unmodified execution with only 1.7–1.9 cm end-effector average displacement error. On RoboTwin, RIFT reaches 92.9% and 92.6% success on clean and randomized scenes, and on real-world manipulation it achieves 45.3% average success, a +6.0 percentage-point improvement over Fast-WAM-Joint.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: World action models (WAMs) are robot policies that condition action generation on predicted future video frames, effectively imagining the outcome before acting. Producing those futures normally requires iterative video rollout, where a video model generates frames step by step, which is slow at deployment. Transformers typically cache key/value (K/V) tensors during generation to avoid recomputing attention, and LIBERO is a standard benchmark of 40 tabletop manipulation tasks used to compare vision-language-action models.

## [LoRD: Post-hoc Calibration for Reliable Log Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17965) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.17965) shows that language model-based log anomaly detectors are severely miscalibrated, assigning excessive confidence to incorrect predictions, especially for anomalous logs under class imbalance. The authors propose LoRD (Log Reconstruction and Distance), a lightweight post-hoc calibration framework that learns prediction-route-specific reliability models from latent representations of correctly classified validation samples and selectively recalibrates high-risk predictions. Overconfident errors in operational monitoring can cause operators to miss real anomalies or waste effort chasing false alarms, so improving confidence reliability directly affects the trustworthiness of AI-driven systems monitoring. The finding that conventional calibration metrics can look good while erroneous predictions remain overconfident exposes a critical reliability gap that standard evaluation would overlook. LoRD is a post-hoc method, meaning it operates on the output of an already trained detector without retraining it, and it selectively recalibrates only high-risk predictions to suppress overconfident errors while preserving reliable ones. Experiments on four large-scale log benchmark datasets and multiple language model-based detectors show consistent improvements in confidence reliability and substantially fewer overconfident anomaly-related errors without sacrificing detection performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Log anomaly detection monitors the log messages produced by large-scale computing systems to flag unusual events that may indicate failures or attacks. Recent detectors use language models to score logs, but a model's confidence score is only useful if it is calibrated, meaning a stated 90% confidence should correspond to roughly 90% correctness. Post-hoc calibration methods use held-out validation data to learn a mapping that adjusts a trained model's predictions so their probabilities better reflect reality, and LoRD applies this idea in a route-specific way tailored to log anomaly detection.

Researchers developed PolyChirp, a TinyML approach that performs multiclass bird species detection in the wild on low-power microcontrollers, moving beyond the previous binary single-species limitation. It achieves robust classification of up to 10 species simultaneously with a macro F2 of up to 0.97, while a data-driven front-end redesign makes on-device mel feature extraction 7x to 11x cheaper. PolyChirp combines biological domain expertise, automated dataset curation, neural architecture optimization, and new hardware with a neural processing unit (NPU). ## [SUN Programs Unify Control and Learning for Multi-Stage Robot Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31167) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Semantically UNified (SUN) Programs, typed executables that compile grounded relations into aligned optimal control objectives, satisfaction predicates, and learning rewards. Their agentic harness Kuafu achieves 82.03% average success across nine multi-stage manipulation tasks and generates demonstrations 10.57x faster than human teleoperation. This work bridges model-based control and reinforcement learning, showing that a foundation-model agent can orchestrate scene preparation, verification, residual RL, and data production while retaining task semantics across tool calls. The zero-shot transfer to physical Franka and Kinova robots suggests a practical path toward scalable, automated robot skill acquisition. The learned controllers produce data that improves visual policy success by 23.6 percentage points over the strongest baseline, and the agent uses program feedback to repair candidate programs and training diagnostics to calibrate relative reward weights. Results are reported on an arXiv preprint (2608.31167v2) without peer review or community discussion.

SUN Programs combine these paradigms by compiling task relations into both control objectives and learning rewards, with an agentic harness managing the overall workflow.

## [Linear Accessibility in Feature Superposition Gets High-Probability Guarantees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09556) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.09556v3) reframes linear accessibility in neural feature superposition as a compressed sensing problem and derives high-probability bounds showing the sufficient dimension scales linearly as d = O_ε(k log m), improving on prior worst-case quadratic limits. It also introduces IHT-SAE, a learned iterative refinement method that improves feature recovery beyond the limits of linear availability. The results quantify the geometric constraints of the linear representation hypothesis, giving interpretability researchers a principled framework for evaluating sparse autoencoders, compositional generalization, and the reliability of feature recovery from neural activations. The bounds are derived for fixed supports under subgaussian noise and validated through Gaussian-tail approximations across system parameters; the analysis characterizes the asymmetry between active and inactive interference as well as the trade-off between interference and observation-noise budgets.

## [EGGROLL Theory: Low-Rank ES Stability and LOO-ROLL Estimator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10980) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper analyzes EGGROLL, a low-rank evolution strategy for LLMs, showing that its mean-field update is a resolvent applied to the gradient of the perturbation-smoothed objective, which can reverse local stability at finite rank and radius, yet still recovers gradients exactly on quadratic objectives. The authors also introduce LOO-ROLL, a leave-one-out estimator that halves estimator MSE at equal evaluation cost and yields matched-time gains of up to 14.1 points on GSM8K and 12.2 points on MATH-500 across models up to 14B parameters. This work provides rigorous theoretical grounding for low-rank evolution strategies, which are increasingly used to train large language models without backpropagation, and the proposed LOO-ROLL estimator offers concrete efficiency gains that could make ES-based post-training more competitive with gradient-based methods. The findings matter for researchers in optimization and LLM training who need to understand the geometric limitations and stability trade-offs of low-rank perturbations. Each rank-one perturbation lies in a zero-volume subset of the ambient matrix space despite having identity covariance, and the additional sampling variance of rank-one perturbations relative to dense Gaussian ES decays inversely with matrix width, reaching only 0.098% at width 4096. LOO-ROLL replaces EGGROLL's two antithetic evaluations per direction with one, and across fourteen post-training settings up to 14B parameters, matched-time comparisons yield eleven improvements in paired tests and no significant loss.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Evolution strategies (ES) optimize a model by sampling random perturbations of its weights, evaluating their performance, and updating in the estimated gradient direction, which avoids backpropagation but traditionally requires dense Gaussian noise that is expensive at LLM scale. EGGROLL (Sarkar et al., 2026) made ES practical for LLMs by replacing dense perturbations with low-rank Gaussian products, often rank one, dramatically reducing memory and computation. This paper studies the theoretical consequences of that low-rank choice, characterizing its mean-field behavior and sampling variance, and proposes an improved estimator.

## [State of Thought Enables Endogenous LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16055) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces State of Thought (SoT), a reasoning paradigm in which an LLM's internal dynamics-geometric state governs how reasoning unfolds, using a lightweight 582-parameter controller on frozen backbones. Across 3 LLMs and 16 datasets, SoT improves mean-baseline accuracy while cutting generated tokens by 62.6% and end-to-end latency by 44.6%. SoT shifts test-time reasoning from externally imposed control, such as fixed reasoning programs or costly search, toward endogenous state-conditioned reasoning, which could make inference both more generalizable and far cheaper. This matters for anyone deploying LLMs at scale, since token and latency reductions directly translate into lower serving costs. SoT extracts a compact dynamics-geometric state from the model's internal information transfer and uses the 582-parameter controller to selectively activate historical reasoning support relevant to the current state. Reported gains include 1.34x on quantitative, 1.62x on general, 1.76x on symbolic-and-code, and 2.51x on long-context reasoning, with 38.2%/36.5% mean accuracy gains retained in training-free/embedding-only settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time compute is an approach that lets a model spend extra computation during inference, for example by iterating, self-correcting, or exploring multiple reasoning paths, rather than relying on a single forward pass. Existing paradigms typically rely on externally imposed control, such as fixed reasoning programs or search over constrained spaces, which can limit generalization and efficiency. SoT instead proposes that the model's own internal state should govern the reasoning process.

## [Matrix AdaGrad: Row-wise and Column-wise Adaptive Subgradient Methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21815) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces an Online Mirror Descent framework with adaptive proximal functions for matrix-valued parameters, deriving Row-wise Matrix AdaGrad and Column-wise Matrix AdaGrad as concrete instantiations. These methods achieve regret guarantees strictly tighter than entry-wise AdaGrad under row-sparse or column-sparse gradient structures, and experiments on matrix factorization and stacked deep MLP training show improved optimization performance and stability at larger learning rates and greater network depths. This work provides a principled theoretical framework for matrix-aware adaptive optimization, filling a gap left by AdaGrad and Adam, whose adaptive scaling is designed primarily for vector-valued parameters. It could influence future optimizer design for deep learning, especially for models with inherently matrix-structured parameters such as embeddings, attention projections, and factorized layers. The framework reveals a trade-off in adaptive scaling: increasing scaling factors reduces the gradient-dependent dual norm term but raises the cost of evolving the proximal geometry. In the row-wise setting, this trade-off becomes separable under diagonal parameterization, allowing each row's adaptive scaling to be derived independently by minimizing its row-wise regret bound; the column-wise counterpart follows by applying the row-wise construction to the transposed matrix.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: AdaGrad is a gradient-based optimization method that adapts the learning rate per parameter, performing smaller updates for frequently occurring features and larger updates for rare but informative ones. Online Mirror Descent is a general framework in online convex optimization that generalizes gradient descent by using a proximal function to define the geometry of updates. Matrix-valued parameters are common in deep learning, but standard adaptive methods treat them entry-wise, ignoring row and column structure that could be exploited for better optimization.

## [MM-ContextFold Folds Redundant Images Out of Multimodal Agent Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23121) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.23121v2) introduces MM-ContextFold, a training-free framework for Multimodal Agentic Retrieval (MAR) that loads raw images only when needed and folds image-dependent subtasks back into a persistent text-only main context as concise summaries. Backed by an empirical study of roughly 10,000 trajectories, the method improves average accuracy by 6.3 percentage points over ReAct while cutting working context length by 27.5% across seven MAR benchmarks and five backbone models. As multimodal agents increasingly invoke external tools, token-intensive visual inputs cause context explosion that raises cost and can degrade accuracy, and existing compression work has focused almost entirely on text. MM-ContextFold offers a training-free way to manage visual context, lowering adoption barriers for teams already running ReAct-style agents and pointing to a broader principle that raw images should be discarded once their information has been textualized. The framework keeps a persistent text-only main context for high-level planning and spawns ephemeral branch contexts for image-dependent subtasks; within each branch the agent loads relevant images, completes the subtask, folds the result back as a textual summary, and discards the images and branch trace. The empirical study found that continued image retention correlates with higher output entropy and can even reduce task accuracy, and the approach is training-free, requiring no model fine-tuning.

**Background**: Multimodal Agentic Retrieval (MAR) refers to agents that solve complex information-seeking tasks by iteratively calling external tools, often following the ReAct paradigm that interleaves reasoning traces with tool actions. In typical implementations, raw multimodal inputs and the growing interaction history are kept in a single ever-expanding context, producing the so-called context explosion problem. Prior mitigation strategies mostly compress redundant text, leaving the management of token-intensive visual content largely underexplored.

## [LBLP: Offline RL Without Bootstrapping or Off-Policy Correction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24489) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Lifted Bellman Linear Programming (LBLP), which enforces in-sample Bellman optimality through inequality constraints in the joint (Q,V) space, so every constraint only involves state-action pairs present in the dataset. Its neural implementation, ALBUM, trains a single critic with a Gaussian policy without target networks or EMA updates, matching FQL's average performance on OGBench while using the fewest parameters and least peak GPU memory among compared methods. This work offers a theoretically grounded alternative to the dominant bootstrapping-plus-target-network paradigm in offline RL, potentially simplifying training pipelines and reducing memory costs. If the approach generalizes beyond deterministic dynamics, it could influence how critics are trained across the broader RL ecosystem. The LBLP minimizer is the unique in-sample optimal pair, and constraints along K-step trajectory segments leave it unchanged for any rollout policy and horizon; under deterministic dynamics it lies between the best dataset return and the optimal value. Relaxing constraints into hinge penalties recovers the same solution above a finite penalty coefficient in the tabular case, and ALBUM detaches K-step rollout targets via stop gradient, with a stationary-point guarantee under a coefficient condition independent of gamma and K.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Offline reinforcement learning trains agents from a fixed dataset of previously collected transitions without further environment interaction, and standard methods fit a critic by regressing onto bootstrapped value targets stabilized by target networks with EMA updates. Bellman optimality is the recursive condition characterizing optimal value functions, and it is known to admit a linear programming formulation in the tabular setting. LBLP lifts that linear programming characterization to the joint (Q,V) space so that all constraints reference only in-sample data, avoiding the need for off-policy correction of multi-step targets.

## [Hill Sampling: Simple Test-Time Scaling Beats Evolution and Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25510) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.25510v2) introduces Hill Sampling, a hill-climbing test-time method that repeatedly samples candidate programs from a frozen LLM, keeps the best program found so far, and conditions all subsequent samples on it. It sets a new state of the art on circle packing among published methods, improves over the AlphaEvolve reference on Erdős' minimum-overlap problem, and achieves strong results on sums and differences of finite sets, using only hours of wall-clock time on eight NVIDIA H100 GPUs. The result challenges the trend toward increasingly elaborate test-time scaling machinery, suggesting that a simple repeated-sampling hill climb can outperform evolutionary search harnesses and test-time parameter learning. This could lower the compute and engineering cost of using LLMs for verifiable scientific and algorithmic optimization, affecting researchers and practitioners building program-synthesis and optimization systems. The paper compares Hill Sampling against what it describes as the largest application by parameter count of evolution strategies (ES) to LLM weights at test time, and finds that learning weights via ES is worse than running ES with a learning rate of zero (random weight-space perturbations). Repeated sampling outperforms both ES variants, and Hill Sampling is strongest of all; the authors recommend exhausting simple repeated edits to the best verified solution before adding archives, diversity mechanisms, evolutionary scaffolds, or test-time parameter learning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time scaling refers to spending extra computation when a model is being used, rather than during training, to improve its outputs; for verifiable problems like math and code, this often means generating many candidate solutions and keeping the best. Hill climbing is a classic local-search optimization technique that starts from a solution and makes incremental changes, keeping improvements until no further gains are found. AlphaEvolve, unveiled by Google DeepMind in May 2025, is an evolutionary coding agent that uses LLMs such as Gemini to discover and optimize algorithms, and it serves as a key reference point in this paper.

## [Vision-Language Refusal Shifts on Image Attachment Alone](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26174) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.26174v2) shows that aligned vision-language models condition their refusal behavior on whether an image is attached to a request, even when that image is a blank canvas that is byte-identical across every prompt. Attaching this uninformative image shifts benign refusal by tens of points, with genuinely neutral instructions moving at most 2 percentage points on three of four hosted models while borderline-benign prompts jump +23 to +51 points. This finding suggests that multimodal safety alignment is not robust to a request property that carries no information about the request itself, meaning the cost of refusal falls disproportionately on benign questions about privacy, self-harm, violence, and illegal activity. It raises concerns for AI safety evaluation methodology and for deployers who may assume image presence is a conservative default they have chosen and priced. The effect is not blanket caution but a threshold shift, and it is not robust on four independent measurements: it varies with canvas colour and pixel count, its sign inverts across checkpoints, it survives an explicit instruction to disregard the image, and on one model it fires on a bare assertion that an attachment exists with nothing attached. On a matched harmful set the same canvas does lower attack success, but the checkpoint with the least harmful headroom, completing only 2% of plain harmful requests, still pays the full benign cost.

The authors argue that a black-box study cannot measure whether attachment correlates with risk in real traffic, but it can test whether the response to attachment is robust to variation that carries no information about the request.

## [Geometry-Aware Hyperbolic Residual-Quantized VAE Fixes Hyperbolic Quantization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26342) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.26342v2) proposes a geometry-aware hyperbolic residual-quantized variational autoencoder that fixes two geometric inconsistencies in naive hyperbolic extensions: non-associative hyperbolic addition, which prevents consistent residual aggregation, and the standard straight-through gradient estimator, which ignores latent-space geometry. The method introduces Hyperbolic Residual Aggregation for the forward pass on the Poincaré ball and a discounted Hyperbolic Straight-Through Estimator for the backward pass, and is evaluated on hierarchical prediction, recommendation, image tokenization, and neural audio coding. Residual vector quantization is a core technique behind modern discrete latent models such as neural audio codecs and image tokenizers, but it has almost always been applied in Euclidean space even though its coarse-to-fine code structure is inherently hierarchical. This work shows that making the quantizer geometry-aware improves the stability and structural organization of hyperbolic residual codes, which could benefit researchers working on hierarchical representation learning and generative models. The forward-pass fix restores the telescoping behavior of residual quantization on the Poincaré ball, while the backward-pass fix routes the reconstruction gradient through the quantizer as a single geometric block to avoid unstable recursive gradient transport across residual stages. The authors report a clear structure-compression trade-off: Euclidean residual quantization remains preferable for pure compression, whereas geometry-aware hyperbolic quantization is most useful for hierarchically organized discrete latent spaces.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Residual Vector Quantization (RVQ) approximates a continuous vector by sequentially summing codewords from multiple codebooks, turning continuous representations into discrete multi-level token sequences. Hyperbolic geometry, which describes spaces of constant negative curvature, is a natural fit for hierarchical or tree-like data because distances grow exponentially toward the boundary, and machine learning models using it often outperform Euclidean counterparts on hierarchical data. However, hyperbolic operations such as addition are non-associative, and the straight-through estimator commonly used to backpropagate through discrete quantization steps is defined in Euclidean terms, so naive hyperbolic extensions break down.

A multicentre study developed and validated a ViT-B/16 model with predictive uncertainty estimation for glaucoma detection from colour fundus photographs, trained on 56,483 images (57.1% myopic, 14.4% high myopia) and tested on 16 independent external datasets across three continents. ## [IROS 2026 Trends: VLA Models, Tactile Sensing, and Humanoids Dominate](https://t.me/ai_newz/4782) ⭐️ 7.0/10

An attendee at IROS 2026 summarized the conference's main trends after visiting poster sessions and talking with authors: vision-language-action (VLA) models are everywhere with 8 dedicated sessions, tactile and force sensing research is booming, diffusion and flow matching are now generating robot motions rather than images, humanoids are abundant (including a competition where they assemble IKEA furniture), and nearly all work uses reinforcement or imitation learning. These trends signal that robotics is converging on the same data-driven, foundation-model paradigm that transformed NLP, with VLA models becoming the dominant architecture for translating perception and language into action. The emphasis on tactile sensing and humanoids suggests the field is pushing toward dexterous, general-purpose robots, which could reshape industries from manufacturing to household services. The attendee notes that data scarcity is the key bottleneck: real robot recordings are still very limited compared to the vast internet text available for language models, and the overall feeling is that robotics is now roughly where language models were a couple of years ago. VLA models typically take a pretrained vision-language model and add an action output trained on robot demonstrations, while diffusion and flow matching approaches capture multi-modality in motion generation.

Researchers developed HapticVLA, a method that uses tactile sensors only during training to transfer haptic knowledge into a Vision-Language-Action (VLA) model that operates without any touch sensors at inference. The approach achieved an 86.7% success rate on three fragile object manipulation tasks: a plastic jar, wafers, and eggs. This work shows that robots can learn delicate, force-aware manipulation without the cost, fragility, and integration complexity of physical touch sensors, which could accelerate deployment of capable manipulation policies in real-world settings. It also advances Physical AI by bridging the gap between rich training-time sensing and sensor-lean deployment. The tactile sensors are used exclusively during the training phase, and the learned haptic knowledge is then transferred to a VLA model that runs on the robot without any touch sensors. The 86.7% success rate was measured across three tasks involving objects of varying fragility, and the project took roughly six months of part-time work alongside a master's program.

## [TGL-NSGA-II Cuts TinyML Architecture Search Cost via Teacher-Guided Surrogates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30553) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces TGL-NSGA-II, a low-fidelity framework for constrained TinyML neural architecture search that combines a short knowledge-distillation procedure (KD-Lite) with a Gaussian-process surrogate to rank candidate architectures without full evaluation. On keyword spotting and bird-call classification, it reports Kendall-τ of 0.74 and 0.62, reduces proxy-score variance by 41% versus random evaluation, and runs 2.2x faster than full NSGA-II. TinyML architecture search is typically bottlenecked by the cost of fully training and evaluating every candidate, so a reliable low-fidelity ranking method could make multi-objective search practical on constrained hardware. The reported speedup and hypervolume gains suggest surrogate-assisted evolutionary search may become a standard tool for edge-AI model design. The framework stratifies samples by difficulty and class, fuses the teacher-guided score with a Gaussian-process surrogate using a derived variance-aware fusion weight, and also derives a capacity-adaptive distillation rule. The guarantees apply only to population-level low-fidelity evaluation and do not establish convergence of the full evolutionary trajectory; selective teacher mismatch raises differential bias and drops Kendall-τ to 0.41.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: NSGA-II is a widely used multi-objective evolutionary algorithm that ranks candidates using fast non-dominated sorting and crowding distance. Gaussian-process surrogates are probabilistic models that approximate expensive simulations, while knowledge distillation transfers knowledge from a large teacher model to a smaller student model. TinyML targets machine-learning models that run on microcontrollers and other resource-constrained devices, where evaluation budgets are tight.

## [S3Q Consciousness Theory Gets a Five-Layer Machine Qualia Architecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30743) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.30743) proposes a five-layer implementation architecture for the S3Q (Simulated, Situated, Structurally Coherent) theory of consciousness, mapping its three necessary conditions for qualia onto existing computational primitives rather than introducing new formalisms. The architecture specifies a single differentiable pipeline operating on per-object slot vectors, plus a developmental bootstrap sequence and falsifiable predictions that no subset of the components produces alone. Machine consciousness research has largely remained at the level of abstract theory, so a concrete, testable mapping from a consciousness theory to a computational pipeline could give the field a shared engineering target and a way to design experiments that confirm or refute the theory. If the falsifiable predictions hold, it would also inform debates about the moral status of AI systems that exhibit such behavior. The paper claims no existing computational system simultaneously implements all three S3Q conditions — grounded sensorimotor situatedness, internal simulation via a world model, and structural coherence between predictions and observations — and it predicts a basic sense of self emerging from linking actions to outcomes, with behavior falling into hesitation, curiosity, or avoidance depending on how unexpected and how positive or negative an outcome is. The work is a theoretical proposal with no empirical validation, and the abstract is truncated, so its depth cannot be fully assessed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: S3Q is a theory of machine consciousness holding that qualia — subjective experiences — require three jointly necessary conditions: being situated in a sensorimotor loop, simulating the world internally, and maintaining structural coherence between what the system predicts and what it observes. A world model is an internal predictive model of the environment that an AI uses to anticipate outcomes, and slot vectors are structured representations in which individual objects or entities are bound to separate, differentiable vector slots. The paper's contribution is not a new theory but an engineering blueprint that composes published computational primitives into one pipeline.

## [BAER Routes Evidence Adaptively for Robust Pairwise LLM Judging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30751) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Backbone-Adaptive Evidence Routing (BAER), a method that selects among three symmetric evidence mechanisms—evidence stacking, reliability-based expert routing, and candidate-blind reference verification—for each benchmark-backbone condition. Across four benchmarks and two 8B judge backbones, BAER achieves the highest test accuracy in all eight conditions, with gains of 0.87–7.32 points over the strongest external baseline. This work shows that adapting how evidence is gathered is more reliable than fixing a single judging protocol, which could improve LLM-as-a-judge systems used for evaluating generative AI outputs. It matters for researchers and practitioners who rely on pairwise preference judgments across diverse models and benchmarks. BAER preserves candidate symmetry by separating each expert's signed preference from candidate-invariant reliability, ensuring that swapping the two responses may reverse the preference but cannot change its strength. The head selection is made on development data and frozen before testing, and the method achieves full prediction coverage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Pairwise LLM judges compare two candidate responses to a prompt and decide which is better, and they can gather evidence through direct comparison, reasoning, or reference-based verification. However, no single evidence protocol is best across all benchmarks and judge backbones, which motivates adaptive routing. Evidence routing refers to dynamically selecting which evidence source or mechanism to use for a given input or condition.

## [PINN/KINN Platform Accelerates Term-Life Insurance Reserve Modelling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30765) ⭐️ 6.0/10

An arXiv preprint (2609.30765v1) introduces an Insurance Reserve Intelligence Platform that pairs a classical Thiele-equation solver with Physics-Informed and Knowledge-Informed Neural Network losses for term-life reserve modelling. The final seven-feature model predicts a standardized reserve ratio and achieved an R2 of 0.9887, MAE of 785.48, and RMSE of 1212.76, with inference roughly 119.53 times faster than the classical solver on 200 policies. Reserve estimation underpins premium pricing, solvency assessment, financial reporting, capital planning, and risk management, but repeated classical calculations are costly in sensitivity analysis, optimization, and large-scale scenario evaluation. If the reported speedup and accuracy generalize, insurers and actuaries could run far more scenario and stress tests at lower computational cost, though the work remains a niche preprint with no community discussion yet. The model uses seven features — elapsed time, issue age, pricing interest rate, scenario interest rate, premium ratio, sum assured, and mortality intensity — and predicts a standardized reserve ratio rather than raw reserve values to improve numerical stability across policies with different sums assured. A key refinement is the use of premium ratio and explicit separation of pricing-time and scenario-time interest-rate semantics, while the authors acknowledge remaining limitations in monotonicity and out-of-distribution generalization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Thiele's differential equation, first derived in 1875 and published in 1910, is a celebrated foundation of modern life insurance mathematics and describes how reserves evolve over time. Physics-Informed Neural Networks (PINNs) are hybrid models that embed governing differential equations as soft constraints during training, while Knowledge-Informed Neural Networks (KINN) inject expert knowledge or rule-based predictions into the network. This paper combines both ideas to approximate the classical Thiele solver for term-life insurance reserves.

## [HasMem: Adaptive Memory Softening for Long-Term LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30797) ⭐️ 6.0/10

HasMem introduces a controller-based adaptive memory softening method that resizes continuous memory entries for frozen LLMs, achieving 95.3 lexical F1 on all 535 questions of a Multi-Session Chat reconstruction probe, a 4.4 percentage point gain over the hard reference. Six configurations with mean per-entry retention around 0.83–0.91 beat rule-based re-encoding by 8.0–23.6 exact-match percentage points under matched budgets. Memory management and context compression are central bottlenecks for long-horizon LLM agents, where ever-growing interaction histories inflate cost and degrade readout. HasMem shows that learned adaptive resizing can outperform hand-crafted re-encoding rules, suggesting a practical path to more efficient long-term agent memory. The method keeps frozen hard-prompt embeddings as a verifiable initial state, then uses a controller to adjust memory widths, a Writer to re-encode resized entries, and Reader/Global modules for readout adaptation and cross-turn state. Notably, F1 gains accompany lower exact match on both evaluations, and on LongMemEval-S local lexical F1 rises from 3.4 to 8.9 while answer NLL falls from 12.257 to 5.274.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Long-term LLM agents accumulate past interactions as text-based memory or compressed context, but resizing continuous memory changes the input seen by a frozen LLM, coupling capacity allocation with readout. Lexical F1 balances precision and recall of token overlap, while exact match requires verbatim answers, so the two metrics can diverge. Multi-Session Chat (MSC) and LongMemEval-S are benchmarks used to probe whether agents can reconstruct or answer from stored multi-turn history.

## [JevSoup: Training-Free Routing for LoRA Expert Composition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30922) ⭐️ 6.0/10

JevSoup introduces a training-free routing framework that selects two LoRA experts using only the input and expert descriptions, then combines them by projecting the second expert's update onto the orthogonal complement of the first expert's row space with equal weights. Across 14 PorTAL tasks and three Qwen3 model scales, it achieves absolute gains of up to 1.19% in task-macro and 1.21% in sample-micro accuracy over the strongest evaluated external baselines, with code released on GitHub. This work offers a practical path toward modular AI systems where specialized LoRA experts can be composed at inference time without auxiliary data, extra training, or autoregressive decoding. If the modest but consistent gains generalize, it could simplify how practitioners build and deploy multi-skill models on top of a single base model. The method separates "System One" expert routing from "System Two" execution, using structured probabilities over expert descriptions to pick exactly two experts, then retaining the leading expert's update and adding the orthogonalized second update with equal weight. Evaluation covers 14 PorTAL tasks and three Qwen3 scales, but the reported gains are small and the approach is limited to selecting only two experts at a time.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that adapts large models by adding small low-rank matrices instead of retraining all weights, making it popular for customizing models to specific tasks. Because different LoRA modules capture different skills, combining them—often called LoRA composition or routing—lets a single base model serve many capabilities. JevSoup draws on the System One/System Two distinction from Kahneman's thinking-fast-and-slow framework, treating expert selection as a fast, intuitive routing step separate from slower execution.

## [AtomWorld-Mem Restores Hidden Memory States for Long-Horizon Atomistic Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31133) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.31133v1) introduces AtomWorld-Mem, a memory-restored atomistic world model that recovers latent dynamical states missing from instantaneous crystal snapshots. It combines spatial encoders that write multi-scale atomistic keyframes with short-term event memory and long-term structural memory to restore a future-predictive evolutionary state, which then prioritizes legal vacancy-mediated events under single-event Kinetic Monte Carlo (KMC) constraints. Long-horizon atomistic simulation is central to studying radiation damage, catalysis, and phase transformations, but snapshots alone are ambiguous because similar local configurations can hide different dynamical contexts. By showing zero-shot transfer across unseen alloy-temperature systems, AtomWorld-Mem suggests that learned memory restoration captures reusable principles of hidden-state inference rather than a system-specific heuristic, pointing toward more efficient and transferable AI-for-science simulation. The restored state only prioritizes candidate events, while event legality, physical execution, and residence-time updates remain governed by the underlying simulator, keeping the approach physically grounded. The abstract is truncated, so full quantitative benchmarks and comparisons against baselines are not yet available for assessment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: World models are AI systems that learn an internal representation of an environment's dynamics so they can predict future states, an idea that has recently seen renewed interest in AI research. In materials science, Kinetic Monte Carlo (KMC) is a standard simulation method for modeling rare-event processes such as vacancy-mediated diffusion over long timescales, but it typically relies on the current configuration alone. AtomWorld-Mem sits at the intersection of these two fields, arguing that long-horizon atomistic evolution is fundamentally a memory-based world-state restoration problem.

AI-augmented engineering means deliberately integrating AI tools into each phase of engineering work to amplify engineers rather than merely automating isolated tasks.

## [Momentum-Guided Federated Split Distillation Boosts Temporal Edge Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31159) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.31159) proposes a momentum-guided federated split distillation framework featuring TeRR-SAtt, a temporal reservoir student attention design that combines fixed reservoir representations, a lightweight temporal student, and personalized output modules. On real-world smart-building data, TeRR-SAtt cuts edge training latency by 65.50%, inference latency by 44.70%, training memory by 18.40%, and inference CPU usage by 33.10%, while the AMGF anticipatory momentum-guided fusion mechanism improves local learning by up to 35.31% in RMSE versus global updates. Edge AI deployments often struggle with limited compute, memory, and bandwidth, and this work shows that combining federated learning, split learning, and knowledge distillation with reservoir computing can deliver large efficiency gains without sacrificing personalization. If these results generalize, the approach could make personalized temporal models practical on constrained smart-building and IoT devices, an area of growing interest for distributed machine learning. The framework has two main components: TeRR-SAtt, which uses fixed reservoir representations to avoid expensive recurrent training, and AMGF, which clusters clients by learning momentum to derive specialized teacher updates. The reported gains are measured against unspecified baselines on real-world smart-building data, and the paper is a specialized academic contribution without community discussion or immediate industry deployment.

**Background**: Federated learning trains models across distributed clients without centralizing raw data, while split learning divides a model between edge devices and a server to reduce on-device computation. Knowledge distillation transfers knowledge from a large teacher model to a smaller student model, and reservoir computing is a recurrent neural network approach that maps inputs into a high-dimensional fixed reservoir to simplify temporal modeling. This paper combines these ideas to support personalized temporal edge intelligence under resource constraints.

## [Neural State Prediction Cuts Shortcut Learning in EEG Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31167) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Neural State Prediction (NSP), a latent-predictive framework that constrains both the prediction target and the available context to reduce shortcut learning in EEG foundation models. NSP pretrains on 2.2 million EEG segments from TUEG and achieves 63.94 macro balanced accuracy across 14 datasets on EEG-FM-Bench, exceeding the strongest baseline by 2.35 percentage points. Shortcut learning is a key cause of generalization failures in machine learning, and EEG foundation models are increasingly used for clinical diagnosis, sleep staging, and brain-computer interfaces. By reducing reliance on low-information positional cues, NSP could lead to more transferable neural representations and more reliable downstream performance across diverse EEG tasks. NSP uses a Target Encoder updated by exponential moving average (EMA) to define latent supervision, applies identity residualization to remove additive effects of channel identity and relative time, and uses topology-separated context to exclude immediate spatial and temporal neighborhoods from visible input. Evaluation spans 30 downstream datasets covering clinical diagnosis, sleep staging, emotion recognition, motor imagery, event-related potentials, cognitive-state decoding, and language retrieval.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: EEG foundation models are large-scale architectures that learn universal representations from heterogeneous brain-signal data using self-supervised methods such as masked prediction. Shortcut learning occurs when a model exploits spurious cues—like stable positional information or local correlations—that solve the training objective but fail to generalize. Neural State Prediction addresses this by jointly designing latent targets and their context, an approach related to EMA target encoders used in self-supervised frameworks like JEPA.

## [Purin Adds Biology-Inspired Synaptic Modulation to CNNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31235) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Purin, a biology-inspired and ANN-compatible mechanism that adds synaptic efficacy modulation to conventional convolutional neural networks using a time-interval-based abstraction of neural activity, avoiding discrete time-steps. Experiments show that after removing confounding factors in AlexNet, VGG11, and GoogLeNet, Purin improves classification accuracy on all evaluated datasets. Most ANNs use fixed trainable weights within a training batch, ignoring short-term synaptic changes that are central to biological computation; Purin offers a lightweight way to capture both short- and long-term efficacy changes without redesigning architectures around discrete time-steps. If it generalizes beyond the three tested CNNs, it could give deep learning models a more biologically plausible and potentially more adaptive form of plasticity. Purin uses a bounded factor to represent temporary synaptic efficacy changes, plus two weight matrices representing input-side and output-side efficacy, with the matrices updated by backpropagation and interpreted as long-term synaptic efficacy changes. The abstract is truncated and no code or full benchmark details are visible, so the scope of the accuracy gains and the computational overhead remain unclear.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: In biological brains, synaptic connections are not static: short-term plasticity can strengthen or weaken a synapse over milliseconds to seconds, while long-term plasticity changes persist much longer. Artificial neural networks typically approximate this with fixed weights learned by backpropagation, and spiking or time-step-based models that simulate dynamics are often incompatible with standard CNN training pipelines. Purin aims to bridge this gap by abstracting neural activity into time intervals rather than explicit discrete steps.

## [G2MAF Refines Frozen Multi-Agent Policies at Test Time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31286) ⭐️ 6.0/10

G2MAF introduces a test-time refinement framework that applies a globally normalized, projected critic gradient to guide and coordinate all agents' corrections of a frozen joint policy. Across 24 MPE and SMAC settings, its canonical variant improves 20 frozen settings, with mean relative gains of 9.2% on MPE and 8.9% on SMAC, while increasing inference latency by only about 6%. This work addresses a key weakness of offline multi-agent reinforcement learning: a frozen policy commits to a single joint action at deployment even when better nearby alternatives exist. By refining actions at test time without further environment interaction, it offers a lightweight, broadly applicable way to improve deployed cooperative policies, which matters for researchers and practitioners in MARL. The method keeps the corrected action both feasible and close to the frozen policy's proposal, using one globally normalized projected critic gradient to coordinate all agents rather than optimizing each agent independently. The reported gains are incremental (roughly 8-9% relative) and the work is a preprint without community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Offline multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) learns cooperative policies from a fixed dataset of previously collected transitions without any further environment interaction, which is useful when online exploration is costly or unsafe. In this setting, the learned policy is typically frozen at deployment and proposes a single joint action that is executed directly. Test-time adaptation refers to adjusting a model's behavior during inference rather than retraining it, and flow policies are expressive action generators that model action distributions through continuous transformations.

## [ReVerPi Study: Completed Pairs Can Hide Capped Failures in Context Projection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31381) ⭐️ 6.0/10

An arXiv preprint presents an 86-run ReVerPi case study showing that context projection cut aggregate logical tokens by 25% while producing bounded success differences, but that completed-pair analyses can hide capped failures when the runner suppresses companion runs after a first-arm failure. Restoring all 27 boundary runs bounds projected-minus-full success between -9 and +1 tasks, and one selector-chosen projected continuation exhausted twelve requests while its full counterpart answered in three. This methodological case study matters because it shows that common evaluation practices—analyzing only completed pairs and aborting companions when the first arm fails—can systematically hide known bounded failures and distort apparent trade-offs in LLM context-management research. It offers concrete recommendations for evaluations to retain every intervention boundary, execute both allocated arms independently, and report completion alongside interaction and token expenditure. In the 15 completed pairs, both arms achieved identical success (12/15 each), and the eleven jointly correct pairs form a fully observed success stratum in which projection reduced aggregate logical tokens by 25% but increased the median pair's tokens by 29% and total suffix requests from 35 to 55. Separating fitting from evaluation changed the selector's apparent tie: outside its four fitting pairs, it incurred one extra failure and 8.6% more logical tokens over thirteen comparable runs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Context projection is a technique in LLM tool-use agents that replaces older, bulky tool observations with compact, addressable excerpts (often paired with a content-addressed handle) to reduce repeated input tokens, at the cost of potentially adding evidence-retrieval turns. ReVerPi is a Pi extension that archives observations and maintains matched full/projected continuations so the two arms can be compared. The study's findings are explicitly scoped to the recorded campaign rather than claiming population-level noninferiority or superiority over unrestricted Pi.

## [New Framework Diagnoses Generative AI's Cultural Impact Beyond Historical Analogies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31482) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.31482v1) proposes a diagnostic framework that decomposes generative AI interventions into three coordinates: the epistemic site where a technology acts, the governing logic by which it organizes its object, and the technical mechanism through which that logic is instantiated. Applying this to information discovery and knowledge synthesis, the paper traces how the shift from indexicality to inference and from editorial authority to statistical consensus produces specific, traceable cultural effects. Policymakers and scholars often justify AI governance by comparing generative AI to the printing press, steam power, or electricity, but such analogies are imprecise about which property of the technology carries the comparison, leading to interventions aimed at the wrong property. This framework offers a more precise vocabulary for distinguishing structural cultural consequences that follow from the mechanism itself from contingent ones open to design and institutional choice, which could inform more effective AI governance. The framework's three coordinates—epistemic site, governing logic, and technical mechanism—allow analysts to separate structural from contingent cultural effects, and the paper demonstrates this by examining two domains: information discovery and knowledge synthesis. It is a preprint with no peer review or community discussion yet, so its immediate impact remains moderate.

**Background**: Epistemology is the study of knowledge—how it is acquired, justified, and held accountable—and 'epistemic sites' are the specific locations or stages where knowledge practices occur. Generative AI systems, such as large language models, are increasingly used to generate candidate programs, proofs, and synthesized text, reshaping how knowledge is found and validated. Historical analogies like the printing press are commonly invoked to explain such technological shifts, but they often conflate distinct properties of the technology, which this paper argues leads to imprecise governance.

## [DeepEdu-v1: Efficient Agentic LLMs for Vietnamese Education](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31568) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced DeepEdu-v1, an AI tutoring system for Vietnamese education built on the SCALE framework, which combines a long-context inference engine that reduces retrieval calls by 7.7x and cuts prefill latency (TTFT) by roughly 35%, plus a self-improving agentic layer that curates a verified playbook instead of fine-tuning. In its deployed configuration it achieves nearly a 2x TTFT speedup over standard vLLM serving and raises agentic accuracy from 70.0% to 79.5% on complex tasks. The work targets a real tension in developing regions: cloud assistants like ChatGPT route sensitive student data to foreign servers, violating data-sovereignty laws such as Vietnam's Decree 53, while self-hosted open models still struggle with memory and latency on consumer GPUs. By showing that efficient long-context inference and agentic self-improvement can run on modest hardware, DeepEdu-v1 offers a template for sovereign, curriculum-aligned AI tutoring beyond Vietnam. The long-context engine amortizes token selection from per-sub-chunk to per-cluster granularity, which is what enables the 7.7x reduction in retrieval calls and the ~35% prefill latency cut while matching or improving accuracy. The self-improving agentic layer is designed to progressively reduce reliance on dominant-language priors as trustworthy local knowledge accumulates, and the strongest per-track gains were on financial-reasoning and interactive-agent benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

On long tutoring contexts this KV cache can grow to several gigabytes and cause out-of-memory failures on consumer GPUs, while prefill latency directly determines time-to-first-token (TTFT), a key user-experience metric. DeepEdu-v1's SCALE framework attacks exactly these bottlenecks, and its agentic layer avoids costly fine-tuning by accumulating a verified playbook of past interactions.

## [Advection-Aware GNNs Offer Little Gain for Solar Ramp Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30286) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A controlled synthetic study (arXiv:2609.30286) shows that explicit advection-aware graph structures do not outperform plain static or learned-adjacency spatiotemporal GNNs for distributed solar ramp forecasting, and that roughly half the benefit of a perfect cloud-motion vector (CMV) comes simply from feeding an accurate motion vector as an input feature. The authors introduce a small self-supervised cloud-motion estimator that recovers the true wind vector with 2-4 degrees median angular error, 2-4x better than classical cross-correlation, and closes about 60% of the oracle-CMV RMSE gap at moderate wind. This challenges a common assumption in renewable energy forecasting that encoding physical advection into graph structure is inherently beneficial, suggesting practitioners may get more value from better motion-vector estimation than from elaborate graph designs. It also provides a practical condition — advection only helps when the advective displacement v*H fits inside the sensor network — that grid operators and forecasters can use to decide when such methods are worth deploying. All claims are established on a single synthetic simulator with a known wind field, so the authors explicitly state that real-network validation is the necessary next step; they also report a negative result for a spatially-coherent probabilistic head. The self-supervised estimator is a position-aware encoder trained only on a multi-lag optical-flow reconstruction objective with an annealed kernel, and it requires no external wind data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Distributed photovoltaic (PV) systems and irradiance sensors spread across a region can experience sudden power ramps when clouds pass overhead, which is a recognized pain point for grid operators. Graph neural networks (GNNs) model such sensor networks as nodes connected by edges, and an advection-aware GNN connects each site to upwind sites with edge time-lags set by the cloud-motion vector (CMV), so a ramp is propagated forward before it physically arrives. This paper tests whether that physical inductive bias actually helps compared with plain spatiotemporal GNNs.

SignTrace is an LLM-powered system that lets users describe a sign's movement in natural language to find the corresponding word in a Chinese sign-language dictionary, achieving 94.0% Hit@1 on a 500-query benchmark. The system integrates LLM-based dictionary enrichment, action extraction, dictionary-style rewriting, seven-channel retrieval, and candidate reranking over 6,699 entries, and has been deployed for user trials with positive informal feedback. By connecting everyday movement descriptions to documented signs, it offers a practical accessibility tool for Chinese sign-language learners and could inspire similar natural-language retrieval systems for other sign languages. Reranking boosts Hit@1 from 71.8% to 94.0%, and the system achieves 97.4% Hit@9 with a mean reciprocal rank of 0.9540; median query-processing time is 13.37 seconds with six concurrent queries. The authors note that dictionary-derived wording and prior selection within the benchmark limit generalization to descriptions independently produced by users.

## [Cost-Aware Best-LLM Identification via Dueling Bandits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30360) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.30360) formulates a cost-aware dueling multi-armed bandit problem for identifying the best LLM among models with heterogeneous querying costs, and proposes a Track-and-Stop style algorithm with prescribed confidence. The authors prove the algorithm almost surely achieves asymptotically optimal cost as the error tends to zero, and validate it empirically on synthetic and real-world datasets. As LLM evaluation increasingly relies on expensive pairwise preference judgments, this work offers a theoretically grounded way to identify the best model while minimizing total query cost. It bridges bandit theory and practical LLM benchmarking, which could make large-scale model comparisons more affordable and statistically rigorous. The algorithm assumes the existence of a Condorcet winner—a model that beats every other model in pairwise comparisons—a condition the authors empirically validate across multiple real-world datasets. It extends classical cost-unaware best-arm identification algorithms and their cost-aware variants, showing consistent improvements in both synthetic and real-world instances.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-armed bandit (MAB) best-arm identification is the problem of finding the best option among many by sequentially sampling them, balancing exploration and confidence. Dueling bandits replace direct reward observations with pairwise comparisons, which is natural for LLM evaluation where human or model judges provide preference signals. Heterogeneous costs arise because querying different LLMs (e.g., large vs. small models) incurs very different monetary or latency expenses, motivating cost-aware algorithms.

## [Large-Scale Study Reveals Key Design Choices for Multimodal Misinformation Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30402) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.30402) presents a large-scale empirical study of multimodal misinformation detection, conducting over 3,375 experiments across three benchmark datasets and a wide range of pre-trained vision and language backbones. The study systematically compares design choices and performs targeted robustness analyses to answer four key research questions about what improves detection performance. As multimodal misinformation becomes increasingly convincing by pairing textual claims with seemingly supportive images, this study provides practical guidance on which design choices actually help, when they fail silently, and which pipeline components most strongly shape model behavior. These insights can help researchers and practitioners build more reliable detection systems and avoid common pitfalls in a field where controlled comparisons have been rare. The study spans over 3,375 experiments across three benchmark datasets and a broad range of pre-trained vision and language backbones, using systematic comparisons and targeted robustness analyses. It distills practical guidance on which design choices help, when they fail silently, and what aspects of the pipeline most strongly shape model behavior, answering four key research questions.

## [Unified Theory Links Concept Formation and Chunk Acquisition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30414) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.30414) reviews Cobweb, a computational model of categorization, and proposes an extended theory that incorporates chunks and their acquisition. The authors implement this theory as Trellis and test it on three synthetic context-free grammars, showing it can represent syntactic knowledge, parse and generate sentences, and learn compositional structures from sample parses. This work bridges two largely separate areas of cognitive psychology—concepts and chunks—offering a step toward unified theories of cognition. It could inform cognitive architectures and AI systems that need to learn structured, compositional knowledge from experience. The theory is modality-agnostic, applying to any experience that decomposes into elements and relations, and uses context-free grammars as a testbed because they contain both concept-like and chunk-like elements. The experiments are limited to three synthetic grammars, so generalization to natural language or real-world domains remains to be demonstrated.

**Background**: Cobweb is a classic computational model of categorization and concept formation that builds a hierarchical tree of concepts from experience. Chunks are familiar patterns of elements that people recognize and acquire as units, studied separately in cognitive psychology. Context-free grammars are formal rule systems used in linguistics and computer science to describe the structure of sentences, making them a natural testbed for theories that combine concepts and chunks.

The paper introduces ContextXR, a benchmarking framework that represents an XR application as a connected graph of functional facets, each a semantically coherent group of capabilities supporting a shared user intent. It also releases MineXR++, a dataset augmenting prior XR interface data with facet-level annotations, and defines three canonical tasks: context factor analysis, initial facet suggestion, and next facet suggestion. Context-aware XR interfaces are difficult to evaluate because current prototyping and user-study workflows offer no systematic, repeatable way to compare adaptation methods across users and scenarios. ContextXR fills this gap by enabling reproducible benchmarking, which could help the XR and HCI communities compare suggestion and adaptation techniques on a common footing. The evaluation protocol scores suggestion methods using a simulated interaction metric based on the navigation and search cost of reaching the desired functionality. Experiments benchmark global popularity, relational retrieval, and LLM-based methods, showing that ContextXR supports systematic, reproducible evaluation.
