## [Aftab architecture boosts replay-free parallelized Q-learning on Atari-57](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07335) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A progressive three-phase design study within the Parallelized Q-Network (PQN) framework compared eight convolutional encoder topologies, integrated Hadamax-style multiplicative feature interactions with explicit pooling, and evaluated categorical-dueling, ensemble-dueling, and categorical ensemble-dueling value-estimation configurations. The resulting Aftab architecture achieves an interquartile mean human-normalized score of 6.592 on Atari-57, versus 2.715 for an independently rerun PQN reference, with a game-level Probability of Improvement of 0.86. This work shows that visual encoder topology, multiplicative representation, and downstream value-estimation design can substantially affect replay-free Q-learning, suggesting that architecture choices deserve as much attention as algorithmic simplifications in this training regime. The open-sourced Aftab framework provides reproducible baselines that could accelerate research into memory-efficient deep reinforcement learning. After completing all architecture selection on Atari-57, Aftab was evaluated on Procgen Hard, achieving a terminal IQM normalized score of 0.418 versus 0.382 for PQN and increasing the normalized area under the learning curve from 0.216 to 0.541, though terminal performance remains heterogeneous across environments. The study compares complete value-estimation configurations rather than isolating the causal contribution of categorical regression, dueling decomposition, or ensembling individually.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Replay-free parallelized Q-learning removes the large experience replay buffers and target networks used by conventional deep Q-learning, relying instead on environment vectorization and network normalization to train stably. PQN (Parallelized Q-Network) is a representative algorithm in this regime that runs efficiently in a pure-GPU setting. Hadamax encoding introduces multiplicative feature interactions and max-pooling before the linear layer, which has been shown to improve model-free Atari performance. Categorical and dueling value estimation are established techniques for improving value-based deep reinforcement learning.

## [VolRouter Recasts Volatility Control as State-Conditioned Routing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10375) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2608.10375v2) introduces VolRouter, a modular framework that formulates volatility control as state-conditioned routing over estimator-controller pairs, using three stages: state inference, switch review, and pair selection. Across S&P 500, Multi-Asset, Bitcoin, and USDT settings, VolRouter achieves the highest Sharpe ratio in three of four cases, improving S&P 500 Sharpe from 0.952 to 1.222 while cutting maximum drawdown from 15.10% to 12.58% and daily CVaR from 1.76% to 1.32%. The work suggests that volatility control can be treated as a policy-selection problem rather than relying on a single fixed estimator or control rule, which could improve risk-adjusted returns for quantitative portfolio managers. It also opens a path for rule-based, learnable, or LLM-based decision modules to be plugged into the routing layer without changing the underlying control policies. Ablation and sensitivity analyses indicate that the gains come from relative policy evaluation and selective persistent switching rather than simply expanding the policy library. The USDT setting is a boundary case where simpler state-aware selectors remain competitive, and the framework keeps portfolio actions generated by predefined control policies even when the router uses learnable or LLM-based modules.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Volatility control is a core technique in dynamic portfolio management: it converts risk estimates (such as realized volatility) into portfolio exposure, often by scaling positions inversely to predicted volatility. Traditional implementations typically fix a single volatility estimator and a single control rule, which may fail to adapt when market conditions change. VolRouter instead treats the choice of estimator-controller pair as a routing decision conditioned on a summary of the current market state.

## [Second-Order Pattern Recognition Proposed for Speaker Recognition Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11182) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (arXiv:2609.11182v2) proposes discovering latent 'second-order patterns' in speaker recognition networks by applying hierarchical clustering to the network's representations of known utterances, then interpreting each cluster with a Hierarchical Cluster-Class Matching (HCCM) method. It also introduces a new task, second-order pattern recognition, and a Hierarchical Cluster Navigation and Assignment (HCNA) method that identifies which discovered patterns also apply to unseen utterances by checking whether their representations fall within a cluster's extrapolation space. The work connects explainable AI with speaker recognition by treating the network's internal context patterns as first-class objects of study, potentially giving researchers a new lens for understanding how speaker identity decisions generalize to unseen data. If validated, the second-order pattern recognition task and HCNA could inform more interpretable and robust speaker verification systems, though the contribution currently appears aimed at XAI and speech-processing specialists rather than a broad breakthrough. The approach applies hierarchical clustering to speaker recognition embeddings so that each resulting cluster becomes a second-order pattern characterizing a context of recognition, and HCCM translates these nested embeddings into semantic labels such as gender, nationality, and identity. HCNA recognizes a second-order pattern as applying to an unseen utterance when that utterance's network representation lies within the cluster's extrapolation space, and the authors report that this extrapolation space substantially improves task performance, though the abstract is truncated and lacks full experimental details.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: In traditional pattern recognition, neural networks are trained to recognize human-defined patterns such as audio categories, while explainable AI (XAI) methods try to explain the latent patterns behind those decisions; this paper calls such latent patterns 'second-order patterns.' Hierarchical clustering is an unsupervised technique that builds a tree-like structure (dendrogram) by progressively merging or splitting data points based on similarity, which here is used to reveal nested structure in a speaker recognition network's representations. Speaker recognition itself is the task of identifying who is speaking from voice features, and understanding how such models generalize to unseen utterances is a central concern.

## [Internal Dual-Wiener Routing Reliability-Weights Distant Gradients in BPTT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12890) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Internal Dual-Wiener routing (Internal-DW), a backward-only intervention that keeps the full forward rollout and all step losses intact while reliability-weighting internal gradient routes in long-horizon autoregressive forecasting. On four history-dominated, weak-drive testbeds, Internal-DW reduces forecast error by 5.2%-13.8% relative to full backpropagation through time (BPTT), and outperforms gradient clipping and Jacobian regularization on three testbeds. This work challenges the common assumption that large distant gradients from long rollouts are trustworthy learning signals, showing that their signal-to-noise ratio can fall even as their magnitude grows. It offers a practical, backward-only fix that could improve long-horizon time-series and sequence modeling without discarding long-range supervision. At each residual block, Internal-DW derives bounded Wiener gains for the identity and nonlinear routes, estimated from route-level gradient statistics and an explicit noise model, to balance preserving predictable signal against suppressing unpredictable variation. The authors note a clear applicability boundary: benefits diminish or reverse when usable history is limited or when the chosen sampler fails to represent dominant drive-dependent variation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Backpropagation through time (BPTT) trains recurrent and autoregressive models by unrolling the sequence and propagating gradients through many time steps; repeated Jacobian products can amplify both predictable signal and unpredictable noise. Credit assignment is the problem of deciding how much each part of the network contributed to the output error, and the Wiener filter is a classic signal-processing tool that minimizes mean squared error by optimally separating signal from noise under known statistics.

## [New XAI method interprets hierarchical structure of speaker embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15203) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This arXiv paper applies Single-Linkage Clustering (SLINK) to speaker embeddings and proposes a new method, Hierarchical Cluster-Class Matching (HCCM), to identify which hierarchical clusters best match semantic classes such as male, UK, or the conjunctive class UK & male. The authors introduce the L-score metric to quantify matching degree and make imperfect matches diagnosable, showing that the resulting hierarchical clusters can be interpreted using speaker identity, gender, and nationality semantics. Speaker recognition networks are widely deployed but their internal representations remain largely opaque, so this work contributes to explainable AI (XAI) for speaker recognition by offering a concrete, quantitative way to probe the semantics encoded in embeddings. If the approach generalizes, it could help researchers and auditors understand, debug, and trust speaker verification systems in security- and privacy-sensitive applications. The analysis relies on SLINK, a hierarchical single-linkage clustering algorithm with O(n²) time and memory complexity, and evaluates clusters with the existing Cluster-Class Matching (CCM) method before extending it to HCCM. The new L-score makes imperfect matches diagnosable, and the interpretation is limited to the specific prepared embeddings and the semantic classes considered (identity, gender, nationality).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Speaker recognition neural networks convert an utterance into a fixed-length vector called a speaker embedding, which is meant to capture speaker identity; systems such as i-vector and DNN-based extractors are common baselines. Hierarchical clustering builds a tree of nested clusters (a dendrogram) by repeatedly merging the closest groups, and single-linkage clustering defines cluster distance by the closest pair of points between clusters. Explainable AI (XAI) seeks to make such learned representations and model decisions human-interpretable, which is the framing this paper adopts.

## [ProtoLIP disentangles object-level visual evidence in VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16284) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ProtoLIP introduces a lightweight prototype-mediated evidence layer that organizes reusable visual prototypes into text-derived semantic families and uses coarse-to-fine evidence routing to disentangle visual evidence at semantic and spatial levels. Across four object- and phrase-level out-of-distribution benchmarks, it reports average relative gains of 29% in Pointing and 43% in Energy, with localization improvements transferring to independently pretrained VLMs. Query-conditioned vision-language models are increasingly used for fine-grained interpretation, but their exposed evidence maps often fail to reflect the evidence that actually drives predictions, limiting trust and debugging. ProtoLIP's ability to decompose image-text matching scores exactly across prototypes, semantic families, and spatial evidence without spatial annotations or backbone retraining could make interpretable grounding more practical for existing VLM pipelines. The method is lightweight and requires no spatial supervision or backbone retraining, and it constructs the image-text matching score directly from localized prototype evidence. On the primary backbone it improves image-text matching discrimination while remaining competitive with a spatially supervised grounding model on object-level localization, though the abstract provides no absolute numbers or per-benchmark breakdowns.

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) process images and text jointly, and query-conditioned variants aim to show which visual regions support a given textual query. A persistent problem is evidence entanglement: sentence-level evidence does not cleanly decompose into object-specific contributions, and object-level evidence can be mixed with co-occurring objects and scene context. Prototypes are reusable visual representations that can serve as intermediate units for organizing and routing evidence.

## [Quality-Constrained Image Coding for Machine Vision Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25108) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.25108v2) proposes a quality-constrained image coding method for machines that caps human-observed visual quality at a target level and allocates remaining bits to machine task performance. It introduces two penalty function variants—an absolute function and a bilinear function with a steeper slope beyond the target—achieving BD-rate reductions of -22.82% and -29.81% versus unconstrained joint optimization and a rate-distortion baseline, respectively. As visual data is increasingly consumed by machine-vision systems rather than humans, this work offers a principled way to balance human inspection needs with machine task accuracy, potentially reducing bandwidth costs in surveillance, autonomous driving, and IoT pipelines. The constrained optimization framing could influence how future ICM codecs are designed and evaluated. The method recasts joint compression-segmentation training as a constrained optimization problem where the codec must meet a predefined acceptable visual quality target while a task term consumes remaining coding capacity. Notably, it achieves these bitrate savings without adding any complexity overhead, and the codec meets the target visual quality with reasonable error.

**Background**: Image Coding for Machines (ICM) compresses images assuming the primary observer is a computer vision application rather than a human, aiming to preserve task-relevant features while reducing bitrate. Just-noticeable distortion (JND) refers to the threshold of distortion minimally perceptible to the human visual system, and is widely used to optimize perceptual compression. Constrained optimization with penalty functions is a classical numerical technique that augments an objective with a penalty term to enforce constraints.

## [Hierarchical GNNs use physics-based graph reductions for power flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26603) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces a hierarchical GNN module that replaces two local correction steps in the GENCO corrective network with information exchanges over two reduced graphs built from Kron-derived transports. Trained with roughly 100x fewer optimizer updates than GENCO's reference protocol, the Kron variant achieves a macro family-balanced voltage error of 0.851±0.110, 51.3% below a per-bus mean reference and 85.0% below the flat GENCO baseline. The results suggest that embedding power-system physics into the graph hierarchy can improve generalization across operating scenarios and multiple grid topologies with one shared set of learned parameters, which could reduce the data and compute cost of neural power-flow solvers. This matters for grid operators and ML researchers seeking fast, reliable surrogates for AC power flow, though extrapolation to unseen topologies remains unsolved. Evaluation used 200 newly generated, preselected scenarios per grid across three topologies, fewer than 1,900 training scenarios per grid, and three initialization seeds; Kron was below the fitted reference on 98.5% of 600 fresh scenarios and 31.0% below the same-anchor Quotient construction. However, on two topologies unseen during training, the current models do not yet outperform the fitted reference in calibrated transfer.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Power flow is the steady-state analysis of how voltages and power injections distribute across an electrical grid, traditionally solved with iterative numerical methods such as Newton-Raphson. Graph neural networks (GNNs) have emerged as fast learned surrogates, and GENCO is a unified neural solver that embeds a corrective network using a heterogeneous graph transformer backbone for power flow, optimal power flow, and state estimation. Hierarchical GNNs add multiple levels of graph abstraction so that local electrical constraints can be connected with the network-wide voltage field, and Kron reduction is a classical technique that eliminates internal nodes to produce a smaller equivalent graph while preserving electrical behavior.

## [Gödel's and Scott's Ontological Argument Ported to Lean 4 and TPTP THF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26806) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper presents a complete, structure-preserving port of the Isabelle/HOL dataset for Gödel's ontological argument and Scott's variant to Lean 4, comprising 30 modules that retain the original section structure, declaration order, and names. A comparison tool certifies that 548 statements are identical as parsed, and every named result the original proves is proved again, including the inconsistency of Gödel's 1970 axioms, modal collapse, monotheism, and the ultrafilter property of positive properties. This work demonstrates both the capabilities and limitations of modern proof assistants by porting a well-known philosophical argument across two major systems, providing a rigorous cross-check of the original mechanization. It is valuable for researchers in formal verification and logic who are interested in proof portability, explicit proof terms, and the interoperability of higher-order theorem provers. Because Lean 4 lacks a sledgehammer and a model finder, automated proofs become explicit proof terms and the 65 Nitpick invocations are reduced to documentation; #print axioms then lists the postulates each proof consumes, showing that Scott's necessary-existence theorem and modal collapse need only symmetry of the accessibility relation, so the modal logic KB suffices. The port also renders the dataset in TPTP THF and SMT-LIB, with six provers (E, Vampire, Zipperposition, cvc5, Leo-II, Leo-III) proving 227 of 294 theorems within ten seconds on one core and 231 within sixty, while Leo-II, repaired and released as version 2.2, matches E at ten seconds.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 29, 04:00

**Background**: Gödel's ontological argument is a formal proof in modal logic for the existence of God, later refined by Dana Scott; it was first mechanized in the TPTP THF format and studied extensively by Benzmüller and Scott in Isabelle/HOL. Lean 4 is a dependently typed proof assistant and functional programming language based on the Calculus of Inductive Constructions, while Isabelle/HOL is an LCF-style higher-order logic theorem prover; TPTP THF is the higher-order logic fragment of the Thousands of Problems for Theorem Provers library, and SMT-LIB is a standard format for satisfiability modulo theories solvers.

The ArGuard shared task evaluated harmful content detection in Arabic memes and LLM prompts across two tracks, with 58 registered teams, 35 final participants, and 27 system-description papers. Best macro-F1 scores were 0.823 on Track A1, 0.419 on A2, 0.984 on B1, and 0.790 on B2, with teams using models such as AraBERT, Jais, and Qwen3-VL. This shared task provides a rare standardized benchmark for Arabic-language AI safety, an area historically underserved by English-centric harmful content research. The results highlight both the promise of Arabic LLMs and the remaining difficulty of fine-grained multimodal hate detection, informing future work in NLP safety and Arabic language processing. Track A2, fine-grained meme classification, was the most challenging setting with the lowest best score of 0.419, partly due to sparse labels and train-test distribution shifts. Track B1, harmful prompt detection, was nearly solved with a best macro-F1 of 0.984, suggesting prompt-level safety filtering is easier than multimodal meme analysis.

**Background**: A shared task is a community evaluation where teams compete on a common dataset and metric, here macro-F1, which averages F1 scores across classes and is useful for imbalanced labels. AraBERT is an Arabic pretrained BERT model, Jais is an open Arabic-centric large language model, and Qwen3-VL is a multimodal vision-language model, all used by participants. ## [OpenAI Halves $200 Pro Limits, Reopens Sign-Ups](https://t.me/ai_newz/4775) ⭐️ 6.0/10

OpenAI has halved the usage limits for its $200 ChatGPT Pro subscription while simultaneously reopening sign-ups for the tier. The company justifies the cut by claiming users will still get more total usage than Pro subscribers had a month ago, and promises not to bring back the old five-hour rate limits. The move signals OpenAI is restructuring its premium pricing ahead of a rumored $500 Pro Max tier, which could reshape how power users and developers choose between AI providers. It comes as Anthropic's newly released Opus 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5 models are gaining strong traction, intensifying competition in the high-end AI subscription market. According to reports, the $200 Pro plan's included usage in Codex and Work drops from 20x the Plus allowance to 10x, netting out at roughly half the old plan's dollar value in API spend. OpenAI says it will not reintroduce five-hour limits and plans to add new subscription features that don't consume usage quotas.

telegram · ai_newz · Sep 29, 07:10

**Background**: OpenAI's ChatGPT Pro tier costs $200 per month and is aimed at heavy users of models like Codex and Work. The company had previously paused new sign-ups for this tier, and rumors suggest a higher-priced Pro Max plan around $500 is in development. Meanwhile, OpenAI reportedly delayed its Astra 6.1 model release over safety concerns, and competitor Anthropic has shipped well-received Opus 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5 models.
